<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{assumption: Boundedness}
    There exist two compact sets $\mathcal{X} \subset  \mathbb R^{d_x}$ and $\mathcal{Y} \subset \mathbb R^{d_y}$ such that for all $k\geq 0$, $x_k\in\mathcal{X}$ and $y_k\in\mathcal{Y}$ a.s.
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- assumption-2: body_md -->
\label{assumption: All Kernel Properties}
    $(\xi_k)_{k\geq 1}$ evolves as a Markov chain on a finite state space $\Xi$ with a transition kernel $K_{(x_{k},y_{k})} $ that is allowed to depend on $x_k,y_k$, that is: for every $\xi',\xi\in \Xi$ and $x\in \mathcal{X}, y\in \mathcal{Y}$ we have: 
    
    \begin{align*}
    \mathbb{P}(\xi_{k+1} = \xi' | \xi_k = \xi, \mathcal{F}_k) &=\mathbb{P}(\xi_{k+1} = \xi' | \xi_k = \xi, x_k=x,y_k=y)\triangleq K_{(x_{},y_{})}(\xi,\xi'),
    \end{align*}
    
    where $\{\mathcal F_k\}_{k\geq 1}$ is the natural filtration of the process $(x_k,y_k,\xi_k)_{k\geq1}$.  We further assume that the kernel $K_{(x_{},y_{})}$ corresponds to a unichain Markov chain for all $(x,y)\in\mathbb R^{d_x}\times\mathbb R^{d_y}$. Also, we assume that for all $\xi, \xi' \in \Xi$ the quantity $K_{(.,.)}(\xi,\xi')$ is $L_{K}$-Lipschitz continuous:
    \begin{align*}
        \left | K_{(x_{1},y_{1})}(\xi,\xi') - K_{(x_{2},y_{2})}(\xi,\xi')\right |  &\leq L_{K} (\|x_1 - x_2\|+\|y_1 - y_2\|).
    \end{align*}
<!-- end assumption-2 -->
<!-- assumption-3: body_md -->
\label{assumption: Lip and Mono of f}
    For all $\xi \in \Xi$, the function $f(., ., \xi)$ is $L_f$-Lipschitz continuous:
    \begin{align*}
        \left \| f(x_1, y_1, \xi) - f(x_2, y_2,\xi)  \right\| &\leq L_f (\|x_1 - x_2\|+\|y_1 - y_2\|)
    \end{align*}
 In addition, there exists $\mu_f > 0$ such that $\overline f$ is $\mu_f$-strongly monotonic in its first argument, \emph{i.e.}, for every $x_1,x_2 \in \mathcal{X}$ and $y \in \mathcal{Y}$ we have
 \begin{align*}
    \langle \overline f(x_1,y)-\overline f(x_2,y),  x_1-x_2 \rangle  \geq \mu_f \|x_1-x_2\|^2.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end assumption-3 -->
<!-- assumption-4: body_md -->
\label{assumption: Lip and Mono of g}
    For all $\xi \in \Xi$, the function $g(., ., \xi)$ is $L_g$-Lipschitz continuous:
    \begin{align*}
        \|g(x_1, y_1, \xi) - g(x_2, y_2,\xi) \| &\leq L_g (\|x_1 - x_2\|+\|y_1 - y_2\|)
    \end{align*}
In addition, for $\mu_g > 0$, $\overline g$ is 1-point $\mu_g$-strongly monotonic with respect to a point $y^* \in \mathcal{Y}$ that satisfies $\bar{g}(h(y^*),y^*)=0$: for all $y \in \mathcal{Y}$:
    \begin{align*}
        \langle \overline g(h(y_{}),y_{}) ,y-y^*\rangle \geq \mu_g \|y-y^*\|^2.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end assumption-4 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
Let $x\in \mathbb R ^d$, $\|x\|$ is the Euclidean norm $\|x\| = (\sum_{i=1}^d x_i^2)^{1/2}$. For $y \in \mathbb R ^d$ we denote the inner product between $x$ and $y$ by $\langle x, y \rangle $. For a square matrix $M \in \mathbb R^{n\times n }$ we define the operator norm $\left \| M \right\|_{\text{op}} = \sup_{\|x\| =1}\|Mx\|$. For a positive definite matrix $L$ we define the condition number of $L$ as $\kappa(L)=\tfrac{\lambda_{\max}(L)}{\lambda_{\min}(L)}$ where $\lambda_{\max}(L)$ and $\lambda_{\min}(L)$ are the largest and smallest eigenvalues of $L$. For a multivariate function $\bar f : \mathbb R^{d_x} \times \mathbb R^{d_y} \rightarrow \mathbb R^{d_x}$, $\nabla_{x} \overline f(x,y)$ denotes the partial Jacobian with respect to the first argument $(\nabla_{x} \overline f(x,y))_{i,j} = {\partial \overline f_i(x,y)\over \partial x_j}$ and $\nabla_y \overline f(x,y)$ denotes the partial Jacobian with respect to the second argument.
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm : mse}
Assuming that A.\ref{assumption: Boundedness}-\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of f} hold, and that $\alpha \mu_f<1$ and $\beta\leq\alpha$, then for any $k\geq 1$ we obtain the following upper bound on the MSE of the fast iterate, 
  \begin{align}
        \mathbb{E}[\|x_k-h(y_k)\|^2] \leq  & \underbrace{(1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k}\left \| x_{0}-h(y_0) \right\|^2}_{\text{Decaying initial condition}} + \underbrace{\alpha \;\left(\frac{\mathrm{B}_1}{1-\alpha \mu_f} + \mathrm{B}_2\right)  + \beta \; \mathrm{B}_3 + {\beta^2 \over \alpha^2 }\mathrm{B}_4}_{\mathrm{MSE}^{x}_\infty(\alpha, \beta)}   \;,
        \label{eq:MSE_x}
    \end{align}
    where the constants $(B_k)_{k\in [4]}$ are fully defined in appendix (Eq.~\eqref{eq::B_1}-\eqref{eq::B_4}), and are independent of $\alpha, \beta$ and $k$: they only depend on the properties of the Markovian noise, the range, monotonicity and Lipschitzness parameters of the functions $f, g, h$. 
Further, assuming A.\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of g}, and $\beta \mu_g <1$, for any $k \geq 1$ the MSE of the slow iterate is upper bounded by 
\begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E}[\|y_k - y^*\|^2] \leq  
        &\underbrace{(1- \beta \mu_g )^{k}\left \| y_{0}-y^* \right\|^2  + D_1 \beta \; k(1-(\alpha \mu_f \wedge \beta \mu_g))^{k} \left \| x_0-h(y_0) \right\|^2}_{\text{Decaying initial conditions}} \\
        & + \underbrace{D_2 \; \mathrm{MSE}^{x}_\infty(\alpha, \beta) + \beta \; \left(\frac{D_3}{1-\beta \mu_g}+ D_4\right) + \alpha  D_5 }_{\mathrm{MSE}^{y}_\infty(\alpha, \beta)}  \;. 
\end{align*}
where the constants $(D_k)_{k\in [5]}$ are also formally defined in appendix (Eq.~\eqref{eq::D_1}-\eqref{eq::D_5}) and depend again on problem parameters but not on $\alpha, \beta, k$.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
To prove the MSE bounds for the fast iterate $x_k$, we first use the strong monotonicity assumption A.\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of f} to extract the following recursion on the tracking errors between steps $k$ and $k+1$,
    \begin{align}
        \label{eq:fast recurrence}
        \left \| x_{k+1}-h(y_{k+1}) \right\|^2 \leq (1-\alpha \mu_f) \left \| x_k-h(y_{k}) \right\|^2 + r^x_k,
    \end{align}
    where $r^x_k$ is a residual term containing the deviation of the noisy dynamics from their stationary means in addition to higher order terms of $\alpha$ and $\beta$ that we detail in the next paragraph. The goal of this recursion is to dissociate the impact of the initial conditions from the cumulative impact of the noise.  Repeatedly applying the above inequality, we get
    \begin{align*}
        \left \| x_{k+1}-h(y_{k+1}) \right\|^2 \leq (1-\alpha \mu_f)^{k+1} \left \| x_{0}-h(y_{0}) \right\|^2 + \sum_{j=0}^k (1-\mu_f\alpha)^{k-j}r^x_j.
    \end{align*}
    This first step is similar to the approach used for the decreasing step sizes in \cite{doan2021finitetimeconvergenceratesnonlinear}, except in the latter case the sum of the residuals under expectation vanishes as $k\to \infty$ which is not the case here due to the assumption of fixed step-sizes.
    
    To bound this sum of residuals, we then use A.\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of f} to show that each $r^x_j$ is bounded by $2\alpha\left \langle x_{j}-h(y_j)  , f(x_j,y_j,\xi_j)-\overline f(x_{j},y_{j}) \right\rangle$, plus a term $M_{\alpha,\beta}$ that is of order $O(\alpha^2+\beta^2+\alpha\beta+\beta^2/\alpha)$. The sum $\sum_{j=0}^k (1-\mu_f\alpha)^{k-j}M_{\alpha,\beta}$ leads to a $O(\alpha+\beta+\beta^2/\alpha^2)$ error term.  The main technical difficulty is therefore to control the sum induced by the first term.  To do so, we follow the Poisson-equation technique used in
    \cite[Lemma 8]{allmeier2024computingbiasconstantstepstochastic} to rewrite the noise as a Martingale difference term, a bounded term that scales linearly with the step-sizes, and a weighted telescoping term. Because of the geometric weights \((1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\), the telescoping term is not exact and leaves additional terms. Additionally, since the kernel is depending on the current iterates, the Martingale term creates a drift in the kernel that we control due to Lipschitzness of $K$ from A.\ref{assumption: All Kernel Properties}. Lemma~\ref{lemma : poisson mse} shows that all these contributions are of the required order.

                                                                                                                                   
    
    To prove the MSE bound for the slow iterate, we apply a recipe similar to the one followed for the fast iterate. From assumption A.\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of g} we obtain an equation for $y$ similar to \eqref{eq:fast recurrence} with a contraction factor $1-\beta \mu_g$, along with an additional term capturing the influence of the fast iterate:
    \begin{align*}
        \left \| y_{k+1}-y^* \right\|^2 \leq (1-\beta \mu_g) \left \| y_{k}-y^* \right\|^2 +\beta \left \| x_{k}-h(y_k) \right\|^2 +r^y_k.
    \end{align*}
    We then use the results of the fast iterate to bound $\left \| x_{k}-h(y_k) \right\|^2$. The treatment of $r^y_k$ is then similar to the analysis done for the fast iterate. The full proof can be found in Appendix~\ref{section : mse detailed proofs}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm : lower bound MSE}
    There exist constants \(C>0\), \(\alpha_0>0\), and a TTSA instance satisfying all the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm : mse} such that, for all step-sizes \(\beta\leq\alpha<\alpha_0\),
    \begin{align*}
        \liminf_{k\to\infty} \mathbb{E} \left [ \|x_k-h(y_k)\|^2 \right ] 
        \ge C\alpha,
        \qquad \text{and} \qquad 
        \liminf_{k\to\infty} \mathbb{E} \left [ \|y_{k}-y^*\|^2 \right ] 
        \ge C\alpha .
    \end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- assumptionb-1: body_md -->
\label{assump : derivability}
    $\overline f$ and $\overline g$ are at least two times differentiable and their second derivatives are bounded operators. In addition, the matrices $-J_{xx}$ and $-\Delta  \triangleq -J_{yy} + J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1} J_{xy}$ are Hurwitz.
<!-- end assumptionb-1 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm : bias}
    Assuming A.\ref{assumption: Boundedness}-\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of g} and B.\ref{assump : derivability}, there exists $\alpha^*$ defined in \eqref{eq: alpha star condition for bias thm}, $\beta^*$ defined in \eqref{eq: beta star condition for bias thm}, and $ \omega^*$ defined in \eqref{eq: omega star condition for bias thm} such that for $\alpha < \alpha ^*$, $\beta < \beta^*$ and $\frac{\beta}{\alpha} < \omega^* $ we have the following bound on the bias of both iterates:
    \begin{align*}
        \max\{ \left \| \mathbb{E} \left [  x_k - h(y^*) \right ] \right\|, \left \| \mathbb{E} \left [ y_k -y^* \right ] \right\| \} \leq& \left \| T^{-1} S^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left ( \left \| s_k^x  \right\| +\left \| s_k^y \right\|\right ) 
    \end{align*}
    for some upper triangular matrix $T$ defined in \eqref{eq:riccati-transformation} and a lower triangular matrix $S$ defined in \eqref{eq:sylvester-transformation}, and where vectors $s^x_k, s^y_k$ are some transformed bias vectors defined in \eqref{eq:sylvester-fast-bias}, \eqref{eq:sylvester-slow-bias} respectively. Furthermore,
    \begin{align}
            \left \| s_{k}^x \right\| +  \left \| s^y_{k} \right\|  \leq & \underbrace{e^{-k c\alpha}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| s^x_0 \right\| + e^{-k d\beta } \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} \left \| s^y_0 \right\| + a e^{-\alpha \hat c (k-1) }\left \| \hat x_{0} \right\|^2 + b  e^{-\beta \hat d (k-1) } \left \| \hat y_{0} \right\|^2 }_{\text{Decaying initial conditions}} \nonumber \\
            & + \underbrace{ F_1(\alpha +\beta) + F_2 \left (  \mathrm{MSE}_{x}^\infty(\alpha, \beta)+ \mathrm{MSE}_{y}^\infty(\alpha, \beta)\right )    + R_{\alpha, \beta} }_{\text{Asymptotic bias}} \;,     
        \label{eq:bias bound}
    \end{align}
    where $F_1,F_2$ are positive constants independent of 
    $\alpha,\beta$ and $k$, whose values may depend on the problem parameters and on 
    $\omega^*$, $R_{\alpha, \beta}$ contains terms of higher order $(\alpha + \beta )^2$. 
                                                      
                             
                                                                             
    Additionally, $\mathrm L, \mathrm G$ are symmetric positive definite matrices solutions of the Lyapunov equations \eqref{eq:lyapunov-equation-fast}, \eqref{eq:Lyapunov-equation-slow}, $c = (8 \left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$ and $d = (8 \left \| \mathrm G \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$, constants $a,b,\hat{c},\hat{d}$ are related to decaying initial errors from the MSE and can be found in \eqref{eq:sequence-sum-mse}, and the MSE terms are defined in Theorem~\ref{thm : mse}.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
Recall that $\mathrm{Bias_k^x} \triangleq \mathbb{E} \left [ x_k - h(y^*) \right ]$ and $\mathrm{bias^y_k} \triangleq \mathbb{E} \left [ y_k -y^* \right ]$ are the bias vectors. As used in \cite{Mokkadem_2006, doan2021finitetimeconvergenceratesnonlinear}, we define the concatenation of the two bias vectors as $b_k \triangleq(\mathrm{Bias_k^x} , \mathrm{bias_k^y})^T$. 
                                                                                                                   
This manipulation allows us to analyze the coupled dynamics and to control cross impacts of both iterates on each other. Using assumption B.\ref{assump : derivability} we use a Taylor expansion of the dynamics $\bar{f}$ and $\bar{g}$ around $(h(y^*),y^*)$ to obtain                
                                                                               
                                          
\begin{align*}
    b_{k+1} = H b_{k} + R_k,
\end{align*}
where $H$ represents the linear dynamics around the equilibrium and the remainder term $R_k$ contains the higher order terms of the Taylor expansion plus the expected impact of the Markovian noise.

As we will see later, the remainder term $R_k$ can be controlled somehow similarly to what we did for the MSE. The intricate part is to find a way to analyze the dynamics induced by $H$ where the off diagonal term represents the cross influence between the biases of both iterates.  To treat this term, we use a linear transformations of coordinates to decouple the fast and slow part, following techniques from control theory, see \emph{e.g.} \cite[Chapter 2]{kokotovic1999singular}. First, we construct an upper triangular matrix $T$ with identity on the diagonal and an unknown upper right block denoted by $U$, such that $\Pi \triangleq T H T^{-1}$ is lower triangular with a zero in the upper right block. This leads to solving a Riccati equation for $U$ \cite[Eq. 2.13]{kokotovic1999singular}.                                                                                            
               
                                   
             
Second, we search for a transform of the matrix $\Pi$ that would make it block-diagonal, by removing the lower left block. To do that, we build a lower triangular matrix $S$ that has an unknown block $X$, and consider $\Lambda = S \Pi S^{-1}$ with the goal to make it diagonal matrix. We do that by solving a Sylvester equation in $X$ \cite[Lemma 8.3]{POZNYAK2008133}. Using both transformations, we get
\begin{align*}
    s_{k+1} = \Lambda s_{k} + ST R_k,
\end{align*}
where $s_k \triangleq ST b_k $. By having the matrix $\Lambda$ being block diagonal, with two blocks $\Lambda _1, \Lambda_{2}$, we can unroll the recursion
               
                                                                      
            
                                                                              
to give us access to individual updates of each component:
\begin{align*}
    s_{k+1}^x = \Lambda_1^k s_0^x + \sum_{j=0}^k \Lambda_1^{k-j} R^x_j ,&& s_{k+1}^y = \Lambda_2^k s_0^y + \sum_{j=0}^k \Lambda_2^{k-j} R^y_j.
\end{align*}
The last challenging part is to find the right norm so to ensure that the leading matrices behave as contractions for small enough step sizes.  This is done by solving a set of Lyapunov equations to get symmetric positive definite matrices that contain information about the linear dynamics of the system and such that the leading matrices are contractions under the Lyapunov induced norms. We can then propagate back the bounds to the original bias vectors by applying the inverse operation $b_k = (ST)^{-1} s_k$. \qed
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We divide the proof into two main parts, the first part is dedicated to the fast iterate MSE and the second part is for the slow iterate.

\bf} 
{Upper bounding the MSE of the fast iterate} We recall that    $\hat x_{k} \triangleq x_k - h(y_{k})$. We also introduce the shorthand
\begin{equation*}
\psi_k \triangleq f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )  - \overline f(x_{k},y_{k}), \quad \Delta^h_{k} \triangleq h(y_{k+1}) - h(y_{k}) \; .
\end{equation*}
Using the update \(x_{k+1}=x_k-\alpha f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\), we obtain
\[
    \hat x_{k+1}
    = x_{k+1}-h(y_{k+1})
    = \hat x_{k}-\alpha f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )-\Delta^h_{k},
\]
so expanding the squared norm gives
\begin{align*}
\left \| \hat x_{k+1} \right\|^2 =& \left \| x_{k+1} - h(y_{k+1}) \right\|^2\\ 
= & \left \| \hat x_{k} -\alpha f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) -\Delta^h_{k} \right\|^2 \\
=& \left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|^2 + \alpha^2 \left \| f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) \right\|^2 - 2\alpha \left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \overline f(x_{k},y_{k}) \right\rangle \\
& + \|\Delta^h_{k}\|^2 -2 \left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \Delta^h_{k} \right\rangle + 2\alpha \left \langle f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )  , \Delta^h_{k} \right\rangle -2\alpha \left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \psi_{k} \right\rangle
\end{align*}
Rearranging the terms to facilitate the organization of the bound:
\begin{align}
  \left \| \hat x_{k+1} \right\|^2  =& 
  \left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|^2 - 2\alpha \left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \overline f(x_{k},y_{k}) \right\rangle  - 2\ \left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \Delta^h_{k} \right\rangle
 \label{eq: decay}\\ 
& +
\alpha^2 \left \| f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) \right\|^2
 \label{eq: noisy dynamics}\\
&  +
\|\Delta^h_{k}\|^2 + 2\alpha \left \langle f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )  , \Delta^h_{k} \right\rangle
\label{eq: high orders terms}\\ 
& - 2\alpha \left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \psi_{k} \right\rangle
 \label{eq: hard term} \; .
\end{align}
We first control the bounded second-order terms. By Lemma~\ref{lemma: bounds},
there exist constants \(\mathrm{M}_f,\mathrm{M}_h>0\) such that
\[
    \left \| f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) \right\|\leq \mathrm{M}_f,
    \qquad
    \left \| \Delta^h_{k} \right\|\leq \beta \mathrm{M}_h .
\]
Therefore, by the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality,
                                                                                                                                                                               
\begin{align*}
\eqref{eq: noisy dynamics} + \eqref{eq: high orders terms} &\leq \alpha ^2 \left \| f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) \right\|^2 + \left \| \Delta^h_{k} \right\|^2 + 2\alpha \left \| f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) \right\|\left \| \Delta^h_{k} \right\|\\
& \leq \alpha ^2 \; \mathrm{M}_f^2  + \beta ^2 \; \mathrm{M}_h^2 + 2 \alpha \beta \; \mathrm{M}_f \mathrm{M}_h \;.
\end{align*}

We now extract the contraction term. By property of \(h\), for every
\(y\in\mathcal{Y}\),
\[
    \overline f(h(y),y)=0.
\]
Hence, recalling that \(\hat x_{k}=x_k-h(y_k)\), we can write
\begin{align*}
-2\alpha\left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \overline f(x_{k},y_{k}) \right\rangle
&=
-2\alpha
\left \langle x_k-h(y_k)  , \overline f(x_k,y_k)-\overline f(h(y_k),y_k) \right\rangle
\\
&\leq
-2\alpha\mu_f\left \| x_k-h(y_k) \right\|^2
\\
&=
-2\alpha\mu_f\left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|^2,
\end{align*}
where the inequality follows from the strong monotonicity assumption
A.\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of f} applied to the first argument of
\(\overline f\), with \(y=y_k\).

                                                     
    
                                                                           
    
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   
                
                                                                                                        
                                                                              
                                                 
                                     
              
                                                                                                              

                                                                                                    
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         
                
                                                                                                        
                                                                              
                                               
              
                                                                
   
                                                                                                                                                                                                             
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
The third term in line \eqref{eq: decay} is controlled by Cauchy--Schwarz and Young's inequality. Precisely:
\[
    -2\left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \Delta^h_{k} \right\rangle
    \leq 2\left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|\left \| \Delta^h_{k} \right\|
    \leq \alpha\mu_f\left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|^2
        + {1\over \alpha\mu_f}\left \| \Delta^h_{k} \right\|^2
    \leq \alpha\mu_f\left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|^2
        + {\beta^2\mathrm{M}_h^2\over \alpha\mu_f},
\]
where the last inequality follows from Lemma~\ref{lemma: bounds}. Combining the previous bounds on
\eqref{eq: decay}--\eqref{eq: high orders terms}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
    \left \| \hat x_{k+1} \right\|^2
    &\leq
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)\left \| \hat x_{k} \right\|^2
    -2\alpha\left \langle \hat x_{k}  , \psi_k \right\rangle
    + \alpha^2\mathrm{M}_f^2
    + \beta^2\mathrm{M}_h^2
    + 2\alpha\beta\mathrm{M}_f\mathrm{M}_h
    + {\beta^2\mathrm{M}_h^2\over \alpha\mu_f}.
\end{align*}
For brevity, set
\[
    \mathrm{M}_{\alpha,\beta}
    \triangleq
    \alpha^2\mathrm{M}_f^2
    + \beta^2\mathrm{M}_h^2
    + 2\alpha\beta\mathrm{M}_f\mathrm{M}_h
    + {\beta^2\mathrm{M}_h^2\over \alpha\mu_f}.
\]
Iterating the previous inequality gives
\begin{align*}
    \left \| \hat x_{k+1} \right\|^2
    &\leq
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k+1}\left \| \hat x_{0} \right\|^2
    -2\alpha
    \sum_{j=0}^{k}
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}
    \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle
    + \sum_{j=0}^{k}
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}
    \mathrm{M}_{\alpha,\beta}.
\end{align*}
Taking expectations yields
\begin{align}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| \hat x_{k+1} \right\|^2 \right ]
    \leq&
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k+1}\left \| \hat x_{0} \right\|^2
    \label{eq: init vanish fast mse}
    \\
    &+
    \mathrm{M}_{\alpha,\beta}
    \sum_{j=0}^{k}(1-\alpha\mu_f)^j
    \label{eq: easy geometric}
    \\
    &-
    2\alpha
    \mathbb{E} \left [ 
    \sum_{j=0}^{k}
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}
    \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle
     \right ].
    \label{eq: poisson term}
\end{align}
The first term \eqref{eq: init vanish fast mse} is the contribution of the initial condition and decays
geometrically. The two remaining terms correspond to the steady-state error induced by the constant
step-size. We first bound the deterministic geometric term \eqref{eq: easy geometric}:
\begin{align*}
    \mathrm{M}_{\alpha,\beta}
    \sum_{j=0}^{k}(1-\alpha\mu_f)^j
    &=
    \mathrm{M}_{\alpha,\beta}
    {1-(1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k+1}\over \alpha\mu_f}
    \leq
    {\mathrm{M}_{\alpha,\beta}\over \alpha\mu_f}.
\end{align*}
It remains to control the Markovian-noise term \eqref{eq: poisson term}. To do so, we invoke 
Lemma~\ref{lemma : poisson mse}, that we detail and prove in Appendix \ref{section: appendix lemmas}.
\begin{align*}
     
\left | \alpha  \mathbb{E} \left [  \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle \right ]\right |  \leq &\alpha \left (   \mathrm{M}_\xi \left ( {3 - \alpha \mu_f \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f} + {\mathrm{M}_f(1 + |\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} ) \over \mu_f}\right )  + {L_{v_f}\mathrm{M}_f (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h)\over \mu_f} \right )    \\
& + \beta \left ( {\mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h + L_{v_f} \mathrm{M}_g (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) + |\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_g \over \mu_f} \right ) 

\end{align*}
Combining the above results provide
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| \hat x_{k+1} \right\|^2 \right ]
    \leq&
    (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k+1}\left \| \hat x_{0} \right\|^2
    \nonumber\\
    &+
    \alpha
    \left(
        {4\mathrm{M}_\xi\over 1-\alpha \mu_f}
        +2\mathrm{M}_\xi
        +{\mathrm{M}_f^2
        +2(\mathrm{M}_\xi (1 + |\Xi|\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K})+L_{v_f}(\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\mathrm{M}_h))\mathrm{M}_f
        \over \mu_f}
    \right)    \\
    &+
    \beta
    \left(
        {2(\mathrm{M}_\xi+\mathrm{M}_f)\mathrm{M}_h
        +2L_{v_f}\mathrm{M}_g(\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\mathrm{M}_h) + |\Xi|\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K}  \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_g
        \over \mu_f}
    \right)
    +
    {\beta^2\over \alpha}
     {\mathrm{M}_h^2\over \mu_f}
        +
    {\beta^2\mathrm{M}_h^2\over \alpha^2\mu_f^2}.
\end{align*}
Using that $\frac{\beta^2}{\alpha} \leq \beta$ because $\beta\leq \alpha$, we are ready to formally define the constants 
\begin{align}
    B_1 &\coloneqq 4 \mathrm{M}_\xi \;, \label{eq::B_1} \\
    B_2 &\coloneqq 2 \mathrm{M}_\xi
        +{\mathrm{M}_f^2
        +2(\mathrm{M}_\xi(1 + |\Xi|\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K})+L_{v_f}(\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\mathrm{M}_h))\mathrm{M}_f
        \over \mu_f} \;,\label{eq::B_2} \\
    B_3 &\coloneqq 
        {2(\mathrm{M}_\xi+\mathrm{M}_f)\mathrm{M}_h
        +2L_{v_f}\mathrm{M}_g(\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\mathrm{M}_h)+ 2|\Xi|\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K}  \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_g + \mathrm{M}_h^2
        \over \mu_f} \;, \; \text{and} \label{eq::B_3} \\
    B_4 &\coloneqq     {\mathrm{M}_h^2\over \mu_f^2}\;. \label{eq::B_4} 
\end{align}
This proves the desired MSE bound for the fast iterate, and allows us to define the non-vanishing term of the bound, 
\[\mathrm{MSE}^{x}_\infty(\alpha, \beta) \coloneqq \alpha \;\left(\frac{\mathrm{B}_1}{1-\alpha \mu_f} + \mathrm{B}_2\right)  + \beta \; \mathrm{B}_3 + {\beta^2 \over \alpha^2 }\mathrm{B}_4 \;.\]

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
