  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Bound of the slow transformed component.}
We now turn to the slow component. Let
\[
    A_y \triangleq I-\beta J_{yy}^{\alpha,\beta},
    \qquad
    I' \triangleq I+XU .
\]
Applying the triangle inequality, submultiplicativity, and Jensen's inequality to
\eqref{eq:sylvester-slow-bias} gives
\begin{align*}
    \left \| s^y_{k+1} \right\|
    \leq\;&
    \left \| A_y^{k+1}s^y_0 \right\|
    +
    \alpha
    \sum_{j=0}^k
    \left \| A_y^{k-j} \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \left \| X \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| R_f(\tilde x_{j},\hat y_{j}) \right\| \right ]
    \\
    &\quad
    +
    \beta
    \sum_{j=0}^k
    \left \| A_y^{k-j} \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \left \| I' \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| R_g(\tilde x_{j},\hat y_{j}) \right\| \right ]
    \\
    &\quad
    +
    \alpha
    \left \| 
        \sum_{j=0}^k
        A_y^{k-j}X\mathbb{E} \left [ \psi_j \right ]
     \right\|
    +
    \beta
    \left \| 
        \sum_{j=0}^k
        A_y^{k-j}I'\mathbb{E} \left [ \varphi_j \right ]
     \right\| .
\end{align*}

We use a Lyapunov norm adapted to the effective slow matrix
\[
    \Delta
    \triangleq
    J_{yy}-J_{yx}J_{xx}^{-1}J_{xy}.
\]
Since $-\Delta$ is Hurwitz by Assumption~B.\ref{assump : derivability}, there exists
a unique symmetric positive definite matrix $\mathrm G$ solving the following Lyapunov equation: 
\begin{align}
    \mathrm G\Delta+\Delta^\top\mathrm G=I .
    \label{eq:Lyapunov-equation-slow}
\end{align}

Since we are assuming B.\ref{assump : derivability}, and we have $\beta \leq \beta^*$ as defined in \eqref{eq: condition beta contraction} that also satisfies ${\beta \over \alpha} < \min \{\varepsilon_{0}^*, \varepsilon_{1}^*\}$ then we can apply  Lemma~\ref{lemma: Lyapunov's slow contraction} to have:
\begin{align*}
\left \| I - \beta (\Delta-  J_{yx}(U - U_0)) \right\|_{\mathrm G}  \leq e^{-d\beta }
\end{align*}
where $d = (8 \left \| \mathrm G \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$, see Lemma~\ref{lemma: Lyapunov's slow contraction} and its proof for full derivation. Equivalently,
\begin{align*}
    \left \| A_y^{\ell} \right\|_{\mathrm G}
    \leq
    e^{-d\beta \ell},
    \qquad \forall \ell\geq 0.
\end{align*}

we obtain, for every $\ell\geq 0$,
\[
    \left \| A_y^\ell \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \leq \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} \left \| A_y^\ell \right\|_{\mathrm G}\leq 
    \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} e^{-d\beta \ell}.
\]
Therefore,
\begin{align}
    \left \| s^y_{k+1} \right\|
    \leq\;&
    e^{-d\beta(k+1)}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}\left \| s^y_0 \right\|
     \nonumber\\
     & +
    \alpha\left \| X \right\|_{\text{op}}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}
    \sum_{j=0}^k
    e^{-d\beta(k-j)}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| R_f(\tilde x_{j},\hat y_{j}) \right\| \right ]
    \label{eq:slow-bias-term-1}
    \\
    &\quad
    +
    \beta\left \| I' \right\|_{\text{op}}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}
    \sum_{j=0}^k
    e^{-d\beta(k-j)}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| R_g(\tilde x_{j},\hat y_{j}) \right\| \right ]
    \label{eq:slow-bias-term-2}
    \\
    &\quad
    +
    \alpha
    \left \| 
        \sum_{j=0}^k
        A_y^{k-j}X\mathbb{E} \left [ \psi_j \right ]
     \right\|
    +
    \beta
    \left \| 
        \sum_{j=0}^k
        A_y^{k-j}I'\mathbb{E} \left [ \varphi_j \right ]
     \right\| .
    \label{eq:slow-bias-term-3}
\end{align}

We now bound the Taylor remainder terms. As in the fast component analysis, there
exists a constant $L_R>0$ such that
\[
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| R_f(\tilde x_{j},\hat y_{j}) \right\| \right ]
    \leq
    2L_R d_1
    \mathbb{E} \left [ 
        \left \| x_j-h(y_j) \right\|^2+\left \| \hat y_{j} \right\|^2
     \right ],
    \qquad
    d_1\triangleq \max\{2,2L_h^2+1\}.
\]
The same bound holds for $R_g$, up to increasing $L_R$ if necessary.

By Lemma~\ref{lemma : Exp Sum Of the MSEs},
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{j=0}^k
    e^{-d\beta(k-j)}
    \mathbb{E} \left [ \left \| R_f(\tilde x_{j},\hat y_{j}) \right\| \right ]
    \leq
    2L_Rd_1
    \left[
        \left(1+\frac{1}{d\beta}\right)\mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
        +
        u_k^{d,\beta}
        +
        w_k^{d,\beta}
    \right].
\end{align*}
At first sight, the factor $1/\beta$ is problematic, however, the term \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-1} still has a multiplication by $\alpha \left \| X \right\|_{\text{op}}$. Since we are assuming B.\ref{assump : derivability}, we showed previously in \eqref{eq:alpha-X-bound} that \[
\alpha \left \| X \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq \beta R_{syl}
\]
with $R_{syl} =  2 \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}$. Consequently,
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-1}
    \leq\;&
    \beta \underbrace{ 2 R_{syl}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} L_Rd_1}_{\triangleq C_{y,1}}
    \left(
        \mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
        +
        u_k^{d,\beta}
        +
        w_k^{d,\beta}
    \right)
    \nonumber\\
    &\quad
    +
    \underbrace{2R_{syl} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} L_Rd_1d^{-1}}_{\triangleq C_{y,2}}
    \mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}.
\end{align*}
The term \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-2} is controlled similarly. 
For ${\beta \over \alpha }< \min\{\varepsilon_{0}^*,\varepsilon_{1}^*, \varepsilon_{2}^*, \varepsilon_{3}^*\}$  we have \[
\left \| I' \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq \left \| I \right\|_{\text{op}} + \left \| X \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1 + R_{syl}(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \triangleq M_{I'}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lemma : Exp Sum Of the MSEs}
gives
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-2}
    \leq\;&
    \beta \underbrace{2 M_{I'}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} L_Rd_1}_{\triangleq C_{y,3}}
    \left(
        \mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
        +
        u_k^{d,\beta}
        +
        w_k^{d,\beta}
    \right)
    \nonumber\\
    &\quad
    +
    \underbrace{2M_{I'}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} L_Rd_1d^{-1}}_{\triangleq C_{y,4}}
    \mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}.
\end{align*}

It remains to bound the weighted Markovian-noise terms in
\eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-3}. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:bias-poisson},
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-3}
    \leq
    \alpha(C_2+D_4)
    +
    \beta(C_1+D_1)
    +
    \alpha\beta(C_4+D_3)
    +
    \beta^2(C_3+D_2).
\end{align*}
We define : 
\begin{align*}
    F_y^{(1)}&\coloneqq (C_2 + D_4) \vee  (C_1 + D_1) \;, \\
    F_y^{(2)} &\coloneqq C_{y,2} + C_{y,4} + \beta^* (C_{y,1} + C_{y,3})  \;, \\
    F_y^{(3)}&\coloneqq C_{y,3} + C_{y,1}  \;,  \\
    R^y_{\alpha, \beta}&\coloneqq \alpha\beta(C_4+D_3)
    +
    \beta^2(C_3+D_2)\;. 
\end{align*}
We wrap all the bounds in the final one
\begin{align*}
    \left \| s^y_{k+1} \right\|
    \leq\;&
    e^{-d\beta(k+1)}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}\left \| s^y_0 \right\|
    +
    (\alpha+\beta) F_y^{(1)}
    +
    F_y^{(2)}\mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
    +
    \beta F_y^{(3)}(u^{d,\beta}_k+ w^{d,\beta}_k)\nonumber  \\
    & + R^y_{\alpha, \beta}
\end{align*}
we merge the bounds of $\left \| s^x_{k} \right\|$ and $\left \| s^y_{k} \right\|$ to get 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| s^x_{k+1} \right\| + \left \| s^y_{k+1} \right\| \leq & e^{-(k+1)c\alpha}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| s^x_0 \right\|
    +  e^{-d\beta(k+1)}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}\left \| s^y_0 \right\|  \\
    & + \underbrace{F_x^{(1)}+  F_y^{(1)}}_{\triangleq F_1}(\alpha + \beta )
    + \underbrace{(F_x^{(2)} + F_y^{(2)})}_{\triangleq F_2}\mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
    +
    (\alpha +\beta ) F_x^{(3)}  (u_k^{c,\alpha}+w_k^{c,\alpha}) \nonumber \\
    &
    +
    \beta F_y^{(3)}(u^{d,\beta}_k+ w^{d,\beta}_k)\nonumber + \underbrace{R^x_{\alpha, \beta} + R^y_{\alpha, \beta}}_{\triangleq R_{\alpha, \beta}}
\end{align*}
We define the new sequence $\mathrm{\hat S^{MSE}}_k$ to be: 
\begin{align*}
    \mathrm{\hat S^{MSE}}_k = \beta F_y^{(3)}(u^{d,\beta}_k+ w^{d,\beta}_k) + (\alpha +\beta ) F_x^{(3)}  (u_k^{c,\alpha}+w_k^{c,\alpha})
\end{align*}
where we have showed that by Lemma~\ref{lemma : Exp Sum Of the MSEs} that the limit exist and it is zero 
\begin{align*}
    \lim_{k\to \infty }\mathrm{\hat S^{MSE}}_k = 0
\end{align*}
which yields our bound: 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| s^x_{k+1} \right\| + \left \| s^y_{k+1} \right\| \leq & e^{-(k+1)c\alpha}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| s^x_0 \right\|
    +  e^{-d\beta(k+1)}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}\left \| s^y_0 \right\|  \\
    & + F_1(\alpha + \beta )
    + F_2\mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
    + \mathrm{\hat S^{MSE}}_k + R_{\alpha, \beta}
\end{align*}
where $R_{\alpha, \beta}$ contains terms of higher order $O((\alpha + \beta)^2)$. Moreover, from the definition of $u_k^{c,\alpha}, w_k^{c,\alpha}$ in Lemma~\ref{lemma : Exp Sum Of the MSEs} there exists $a,b, \hat c, \hat d$ such that:
\begin{align}
    \mathrm{\hat S^{MSE}}_k\leq a e^{-\alpha \hat c k }\left \| \hat x_{0} \right\|^2 + b  e^{-\beta \hat d k } \left \| \hat y_{0} \right\|^2\label{eq:sequence-sum-mse}
\end{align}
So our final bound will be
\begin{align*}
    \left \| s^x_{k+1} \right\| + \left \| s^y_{k+1} \right\| \leq & e^{-(k+1)c\alpha}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| s^x_0 \right\|
    +  e^{-d\beta(k+1)}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)}\left \| s^y_0 \right\| + a e^{-\alpha \hat c k }\left \| \hat x_{0} \right\|^2 + b  e^{-\beta \hat d k } \left \| \hat y_{0} \right\|^2  \\
    & + F_1(\alpha + \beta )
    + F_2\mathcal M_{\alpha,\beta}
    + R_{\alpha, \beta}
\end{align*}

                         
 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex}

\@xpt\@xipt

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Unwrapping the results.} It remains to return to the original bias coordinates. By construction,
\[
    s_k
    =
    \left [ \begin{array}{c}
        s^x_{k}  \\
        s^y_{k}
\end{array} \right ]
    =
    S T \left [ \begin{array}{c}
         b^x_{k}  \\
         b^y_{k}
    \end{array} \right ].
\]
Since the matrices $S$ and $T$ are invertible, we can write equivalently,
\[
    \left [ \begin{array}{c}
         b^x_{k}  \\
         b^y_{k}
    \end{array} \right ]
    =
    T^{-1}S^{-1}s_k .
\]
Therefore, by applying the norm on both sides,
\begin{align*}
    \left \| \left [ \begin{array}{c}
         b^x_{k}  \\
         b^y_{k}
    \end{array} \right ] \right\|
    &\leq
    \left \| T^{-1}S^{-1}s_k \right\|
    \\
    &\leq
    \left \| T^{-1}S^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| s_k \right\|
    \\
    &\leq
    \left \| T^{-1}S^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \left(
        \left \| s_k^x \right\|+\left \| s_k^y \right\|
    \right).
\end{align*}
where in the last inequality we have used the triangular inequality since 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| \left [ \begin{array}{c}
        s^x_{k}  \\
        s^y_{k}
\end{array} \right ] \right\|  = \left \|  \left [ \begin{array}{c} 
         s_k^x \\
         0
    \end{array} \right ] +  \left [ \begin{array}{c} 
         0 \\
         s_k^y 
    \end{array} \right ] \right\| \leq \left \| s_k^x \right\|+ \left \| s_k^y \right\|
\end{align*}
Plugging the bounds obtained above for the fast and slow
transformed components gives the desired bias bound:
\[
    \max\left\{
        \left \| b_k^x \right\|,
        \left \| {b_k^y} \right\|
    \right\}
    \leq
    \left \| T^{-1}S^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}
    \left(
        \left \| s_k^x \right\|+\left \| s_k^y \right\|
    \right).
\]
which concludes the proof of our theorem.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lemma: bounds}
Under assumptions A.\ref{assumption: Boundedness}-\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of g}, there exist finite non-negative constants $\mathrm{M}_f, \mathrm{M}_g, \mathrm{M}_h, \mathrm{M}_\xi,\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}$ dependent on the problem settings satisfying :
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $\|\overline f(x_{k},y_{k})\| \leq 2L_f\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\|\overline f(0,0)\|\leq \mathrm{M}_f$
    \item $\|\overline g(x_{k},y_{k})\| \leq 2L_g\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+ \|\overline g(0,0)\|\leq \mathrm{M}_g$
    \item $\|f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\| \leq 2L_f\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \leq \mathrm{M}_f$
    \item $\|g(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\| \leq  2L_g\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+ \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \leq \mathrm{M}_g$
    \item $\|\Delta^h_{k}\| \leq \beta L_h\mathrm{M}_g \leq \beta \mathrm{M}_h$
    \item $\|\psi_k\| \leq \mathrm{M}_\xi$ 
    \item $\|h(y_{k})\| \leq L_h \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} + \left \| h(y_{0}) \right\|\leq \mathrm{M}_h$
    \item Given assumption A.\ref{assumption: All Kernel Properties}-\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of g}, for every $x_1, x_2 \in \mathbb R^{d_x} , y_1, y_2 \in \mathbb R^{d_y}$, $v_f$ and $v_g$ (as defined in \eqref{eq: poisson solution fast} and \eqref{eq: poisson solution slow}) are $L_{v_f}$ and $L_{v_g}$-Lipschitz continuous:
    \begin{align*}
        \| v_f(x_1,y_1,\xi)- v_f(x_2,y_2,\xi)\|\leq L_{v_f}  (\|x_1-x_2\|+\|y_1 -y_2\|)\\
        \| v_g(x_1,y_1,\xi)- v_g(x_2,y_2,\xi)\|\leq L_{v_g}  (\|x_1-x_2\|+\|y_1 -y_2\|)
    \end{align*}
    \item $\|v_f(x_1,y_1,\xi)\|\leq  2L_f\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} + f_0 \leq \mathrm{M}_\xi $
    \item $\left \langle \hat x_{k}  , v_f(x_k,y_k,\xi_{k+1})  \right\rangle\leq  \mathrm{M}_\xi$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
We provide one line proof for each point:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $\|\overline f(x_{k},y_{k})\| \leq \|\overline f(x_{k},y_{k}) - \overline f(0,0)\| + \|\overline f(0,0)\|\leq L_f (\|x_k\|+\|y_k\|) + \|\overline f(0,0)\| \leq 2L_f\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\|\overline f(0,0)\|\leq \mathrm{M}_f$
    \item $\|\overline g(x_{k},y_{k})\| \leq 2L_g\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+ \|\overline g(0,0)\|\leq \mathrm{M}_g$
    \item $\|f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\| \leq 2L_f\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \leq \mathrm{M}_f$
    \item $\|g(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\| \leq  2L_g\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}+ \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \leq \mathrm{M}_g$
    \item $\|\Delta^h_{k}\|=\|h(y_{k+1})-h(y_{k}))\|\leq L_h \|\beta g(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\|\leq \beta  \mathrm{M}_h$
    \item $\|\psi_k\| = \|f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) - \overline f(x_{k},y_{k})\| \leq \|f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )\| + \|\overline f(x_{k},y_{k})\| \leq 2 \mathrm{M}_f \leq  \mathrm{M}_\xi$ 
    \item $\|h(y_{k})\| \leq L_h \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} + \left \| h(y_{0}) \right\| \leq \mathrm{M}_h$
    \item See \cite[Lemma 8]{allmeier2024computingbiasconstantstepstochastic} and \cite[Lemma D.4]{haque2025tightfinitetimebounds}.
    \item $\|v_f(x_1,y_1,\xi)\|\leq  2L_f\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} + f_0 \leq  \mathrm{M}_\xi $
    \item $\left \langle \hat x_{k}  ,  v_f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{{k}+1}) \right\rangle\leq \left \| \hat x_{k} \right\| \left \| v_f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{{k}+1}) \right\| \leq \mathrm{M}_\xi$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lemma : poisson mse}
Taking the sequence $(x_k,y_k)_{k\geq 0}$ defined by \eqref{eq: TTSA update} and under assumptions A.\ref{assumption: Boundedness}-\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of f}, for every $\alpha  \mu_f <1 $ and $\beta \mu_g <1$:
    \begin{align*} 
        
\left | \alpha  \mathbb{E} \left [  \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle \right ]\right |  \leq &\alpha \left (   \mathrm{M}_\xi \left ( {3 - \alpha \mu_f \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f} + {\mathrm{M}_f(1 + |\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} ) \over \mu_f}\right )  + {L_{v_f}\mathrm{M}_f (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h)\over \mu_f} \right )    \\
& + \beta \left ( {\mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h + L_{v_f} \mathrm{M}_g (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) + |\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_g \over \mu_f} \right ) 
  \\
                \left | \beta  \mathbb{E} \left [  \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , \phi_j \right\rangle \right ]\right |  \leq&  \beta \left (  {(L_{v_g} + \mathrm{M}_\xi  L_{K}  |\Xi|) \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_g  \over \mu_g} +   \mathrm{M}_\xi \left ( {3 - \beta \mu_g \over 1 - \beta \mu_g} + {\mathrm{M}_g \over \mu_g} \right ) \right )  \\
        & + \alpha {(L_{v_g} + \mathrm{M}_\xi  L_{K}  |\Xi|) \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_f\over \mu_g}
    \end{align*}
    where $\psi_k = f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} )  - \overline f(x_{k},y_{k})$ and $\phi_k = g(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) - \overline g(x_{k},y_{k})$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
To prove this lemma, we use the same technique in \cite[Lemma 8]{allmeier2024computingbiasconstantstepstochastic} and \cite{kaledin2020finite} by invoking the solution of a Poisson equation that allow us to decompose the deviation terms $f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )-\overline f(x_{j},y_{j})$ inside the sum into a Martingale Difference Sequence (MDS), a bounded term and a telescoping term. Since our terms in the sum are weighted by $(1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}$ we don't have exactly the telescoping terms that we want, instead, we have additional residuals that we needed to deal with. Moreover, since our transition kernel $K$ is depending on the iterates, we will have a drift between the Poisson solution time step and the required term to build the Martingale Difference. In this proof we first start by introducing the core idea of the Poisson equation, then we derive each component individually to get their respective bounds. Moreover, throught this lemma we bound images of the functions $g$ and $\bar g$ by continuity and compactness and not using A.~\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of g} and we use the same upper bound constant $\mathrm{M}_g$. 
Under assumption A.\ref{assumption: All Kernel Properties}, by \cite[Definition 21.2.1]{MC_2018_moulines} and \cite[Lemma 8]{allmeier2024computingbiasconstantstepstochastic} $v_f$ is a solution of the following Poisson equation:

\begin{align}
    v_f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{{k}+1}) - \sum_{\xi'\in \Xi}K_{(x_{k},y_{k})}(\xi_{k+1}, \xi' ) v_f(x_k,y_k,\xi') = f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi_{k +1} ) - \overline f(x_{k},y_{k}) \label{eq: poisson solution fast}
\end{align}
In the following, we use the notation, with $K_k \triangleq  K_{(x_{k},y_{k})}$ \[\mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{k}(\xi_{{k+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{k},y_{k},\xi')] \triangleq \sum_{\xi'\in \Xi}K_{(x_{k},y_{k})}(\xi_{k+1}, \xi' )  v_f(x_k,y_k,\xi').\] 
It is shown in \cite[Lemma 8]{allmeier2024computingbiasconstantstepstochastic} that $v_f$ is Lipschitz-continuous. Our goal from introducing this solution is to be able to write this decomposition: 
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle =\sum_{j=0}^{k} \text{MDS + Telescoping Term+ Bounded Term + Kernel Drift}
\end{align*}
First, we start by substituting \eqref{eq: poisson solution fast} in the sum:
\begin{align*}
 \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle =\sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat x_{j}  ,  v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]] \right\rangle
\end{align*}
We notice that the term $\mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]$ does not make a Martingale Difference with the term $v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1})$ since $\xi'$ is sampled at the step $j+2$, we add and subtract $v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+2}})$:
\begin{align*}
v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+1}}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] =& v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+2}}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \\
& + v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+1}}) - v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+2}})
\end{align*}
However, this is not enough since $\xi_{j+2}\sim K_{(x_{j+1},y_{j+1})}(\xi_{j+1}, \cdot)$ and not $K_{(x_{j},y_{j})}(\xi_{j+1}, \cdot)$. To obtain a Martingale Difference we add and subtract $\mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]$. Finally, to use Lipschitzness of $v_f$ and get a telescoping term we add and subtract $v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{{j+2}})$. This will give us the following decomposition of the Markovian noise deviation
\begin{align}
    \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle = \Big{\langle} \hat x_{j},&v_f(x_j,y_j,\xi_{j+2}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]\label{eq: poisson fast 1}\\
    & + v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) - v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j+2})\label{eq: poisson fast 2}\\  
    & +v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1}) - v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) \label{eq: poisson fast 3}\\
    & + \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]-\mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] 
    \Big{\rangle}\label{eq: poisson fast 4}
\end{align}
the first term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 1} is an MDS, so it reduces to zero under expectation: 
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E} \left [  \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat x_{j}  ,  v_f(x_j,y_j,\xi_{j+2}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \right\rangle \right ] = 0
\end{align*}
The second term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 2} scales linearly with the sum of step-sizes, since $f$ and $K$ are Lipschitz, so $v_f$ will be $L_{v_f}$-Lipschitz too for some positive constant $L_{v_f}$ and this is due to Lemma \ref{lemma: bounds}. So we can bound \eqref{eq: poisson fast 2} by:
\begin{align*}
     \left | \eqref{eq: poisson fast 2}\right | 
     \leq &  \left \| \hat x_{j} \right\| \left \|  v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) - v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j+2}) \right\|\\
     = & \left \| x_j - h(y_j) \right\| \left \|  v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) - v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j+2}) \right\|\\
     \leq & L_{v_f} \left \| x_j - h(y_j) \right\| (\left \| x_{j+1} -x_j \right\| + \left \| y_{j+1} - y_j \right\|)\\
     \leq & L_{v_f} (\left \| x_j \right\| +\left \| h(y_j) \right\| )(\left \| x_{j+1} -x_j \right\| + \left \| y_{j+1} - y_j \right\|)\\
     \leq & L_{v_f} (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) ( \left \| \alpha f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  \right\| + \left \| \beta g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  \right\|)\\
     \leq & L_{v_f} (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) ( \alpha \mathrm{M}_f +  \beta \mathrm{M}_g )
\end{align*}
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                          
                
                                       
                                                                                                  
                                                                                                      
                                                                                           
                                                                                                   
                                                                                        
                                                                            
               
For brevity we set $z_j = \left \langle \hat x_{j}  ,  v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) - v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j+2}) \right\rangle$. Now we apply our bound on the sum to have 
\begin{align*}
    \left | \alpha\sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}z_j\right |  \leq & \alpha\sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\left | z_j\right |  \\  \leq & \alpha\sum_{j=0}^{k}(1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}L_{v_f} (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) ( \alpha \mathrm{M}_f +  \beta \mathrm{M}_g )\\
    = & \alpha L_{v_f} (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) ( \alpha \mathrm{M}_f +  \beta \mathrm{M}_g ) \left ( 1 - (1-\alpha \mu_f)^{k+1} \over \mu_f \alpha \right ) \\
    \leq &  {L_{v_f} (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h)\over \mu_f} ( \alpha \mathrm{M}_f +  \beta \mathrm{M}_g )
\end{align*}
 To see why the term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 4} is bounded we use our assumptions  A~\ref{assumption: All Kernel Properties} and A~\ref{assumption: Lip and Mono of f} about Lipschitzness of $K$ and $f$ 
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq: poisson fast 4} &= \left \| \sum_{\xi' \in \Xi} (K_{j+1}(\xi_{j+1}, \xi') - K_{j}(\xi_{j+1}, \xi'))v_f(x_j,y_j,\xi') \right\|\\
    & \leq \sum_{\xi' \in \Xi} \left \| K_{j+1}(\xi_{j+1},\xi') - K_{j}(\xi_{j+1}, \xi') \right\|\left \|  v_f(x_j,y_j,\xi') \right\|\\
    & \leq \sum_{\xi' \in \Xi} L_{K} \left ( \left \| x_{j+1} - x_j \right\|+ \left \| y_{j+1} - y_j \right\|\right )  \mathrm{M}_\xi\\
    & \leq L_{K} \mathrm{M}_\xi(\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g) |\Xi|
\end{align*}
which gives the bound 
\begin{align*}
    \alpha \left |  \sum_{j=0}^k(1-\mu_f \alpha)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]-\mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \right\rangle\right |  \\ \leq {|\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} \mathrm{M}_\xi(\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g)  \over \mu_f}
\end{align*}

Finally, we handle the third term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 3} which is supposed to be a telescoping term inside the sum:
\begin{align}
      \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  ,  v_f(x_j,y_j,\xi_{j+1}) - v_f(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) \right\rangle \label{eq: main poisson sum for x}
\end{align}
This is not perfectly telescoping. This hurdle emerges from two quantities: first, in the inner product itself we have $\hat x_{j}$ which depends on $j$ and second, the weights $(1-\alpha \mu_f)^{k-j}$ also depend on $j$. To get over this, we will proceed in two steps. First, we fix the telescoping term inside the inner product by using the update rule in \eqref{eq: TTSA update}. Due to this step, we will have a remainder term dependent on the dynamics of $x_j$ and controlled by $\alpha$. The second step, is to handle the weights to have our telescoping terms. These two steps, will introduce remainders that we will need to control. Now we detail every step, so we start with the inner product and we set $v_j = v_f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1})$ so we can easily write the computations:
\begin{align*}
    \left \langle \hat x_{j}  ,  v_{j+1} - v_{j} \right\rangle 
    & = \left \langle x_j- h(y_{j})  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle - \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle\\
    &= \left \langle x_{j+1} + \alpha f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} ) - h(y_{j})  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle -\left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle\\
    & = \left \langle \hat x_{j+1} + \alpha f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} ) + \Delta^h_{j}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle -\left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle\\
    & = \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle  - \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle + \left \langle \alpha f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} ) +\Delta^h_{j}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle
\end{align*}
where $\Delta^h_{j}= h(y_{j+1})-h(y_{j})$. Now, we have a telescoping term plus a remainder. Substituting the last line into \eqref{eq: main poisson sum for x}: 
\begin{align}
    \eqref{eq: main poisson sum for x} &=  \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}( \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle  - \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle  ) \label{eq: almost telescoping}\\ 
     &+ \alpha^2 \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}  \left \langle f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle \nonumber\\
     &+ \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \Delta^h_{j}  ,  v_{j+1} \right\rangle \nonumber
\end{align}
Starting with the first term \eqref{eq: almost telescoping}. Because of the weights in the sum $(1-\alpha \mu_f)^{k-j}$ we can't directly have a telescoping sum. An extra step has to be done for this term to be able to make it telescope with the cost of introducing new remainders. Hopefully, these remainders can be controlled. We proceed by:
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq: almost telescoping}=& \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle  - \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle    \\
     =& \alpha (1-\alpha\mu_f)\sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-(j+1)} \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle  -\alpha  \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle \\ 
    =& \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-(j+1)} \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle  - \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , v_{j} \right\rangle \\
    & -\alpha^2\mu_f\sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-(j+1)} \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle\\ 
    = & \alpha (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{-1}\left \langle \hat x_{k+1}  , v_{k+1} \right\rangle - \alpha (1-\alpha\mu_f)^k \left \langle \hat x_{0}  , v_{0} \right\rangle  \\
    &-\alpha^2\mu_f\sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-(j+1)} \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle
\end{align*}
where in the second equality we factored out $1-\alpha \mu_f$ to make the inside weight match the weight in the other sum. In the last line, we removed the telescoping terms to end up with the final three terms. Now, into \eqref{eq: main poisson sum for x} we apply absolute value to get: 
\begin{align}
   \left | \eqref{eq: main poisson sum for x}\right |  \leq & \left | \alpha \Big((1-\alpha\mu_f)^{-1}\left \langle \hat x_{k+1}  , v_{k+1} \right\rangle- (1-\alpha\mu_f)^k \left \langle \hat x_{0}  , v_{0} \right\rangle \Big) \label{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 1}\right |  \\
   &+ \alpha \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j}\left |  \left \langle \Delta^h_{j}  ,  v_{j+1} \right\rangle \right | \label{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 2}\\
    &  +\alpha^2\mu_f\sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-(j+1)} \left |  \left \langle \hat x_{j+1}  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle\right |  \label{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 3}\\ 
    &+ \alpha^2 \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left |  \left \langle f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  , v_{j+1} \right\rangle\right |  \label{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 4}
\end{align}
We bound each term independently. Starting with the first term \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 1}: 
                                                                                                                                         
                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       
                
                            
              
   
                                                                                  
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 1} \leq \alpha{\mathrm{M}_\xi \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f} + \alpha {\mathrm{M}_\xi  } = \alpha \mathrm{M}_\xi \left (  2 -\alpha \mu_f \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f\right ) 
\end{align*}
the second term \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 2}
\begin{align*}
   \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 2} &\leq \alpha\sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \|\Delta^h_{j}\|\|v_{j+1}\| \leq  \alpha  \beta \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{j} \\
     & = \alpha \beta \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h {1 - (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k+1} \over \alpha \mu_f} \leq \beta {  \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h \over \mu_f} 
\end{align*}
the third term \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 3}
\begin{align*}
   \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 3} &\leq   \alpha ^2 \mu_f \mathrm{M}_\xi \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-(j+1)}  =  \alpha ^2 \mu_f \mathrm{M}_\xi (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \\
    & \leq \alpha {\mathrm{M}_\xi \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f}
\end{align*}
and the last term \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 4}
\begin{align*}
     \eqref{eq: fast poisson telescoping remainder 4}&\leq \alpha^2 \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_f \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{j} \leq  \alpha { \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_f \over \mu_f} 
\end{align*}
After bounding each term individually, using bounds from Lemma~\ref{lemma: bounds}, we apply these bounds on the main sum \eqref{eq: main poisson sum for x}
\begin{align*}
    \left | \eqref{eq: main poisson sum for x} \right | \leq  \alpha \mathrm{M}_\xi \left (  {3 - \alpha \mu_f \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f} + {\mathrm{M}_f\over \mu_f}\right )    + \beta {  \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h \over \mu_f} 
\end{align*}
Since expectation preserves order, we apply it on both sides to get the final result:
\begin{align*}

\left | \alpha  \mathbb{E} \left [  \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\alpha\mu_f)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat x_{j}  , \psi_j \right\rangle \right ]\right |  \leq &\alpha \left (   \mathrm{M}_\xi \left ( {3 - \alpha \mu_f \over 1 - \alpha \mu_f} + {\mathrm{M}_f(1 + |\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} ) \over \mu_f}\right )  + {L_{v_f}\mathrm{M}_f (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h)\over \mu_f} \right )    \\
& + \beta \left ( {\mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_h + L_{v_f} \mathrm{M}_g (\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} +  \mathrm{M}_h) + |\Xi| \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_g \over \mu_f} \right ) 

\end{align*}
which concludes the proof of the first part.\\
As above, let $v_g$ be the solution of the Poisson equation:
                                                                                                                                
    \begin{align}
      g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} ) - \overline g(x_{j},y_{j})  =  v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]  
    \label{eq: poisson solution slow}
\end{align}
we rewrite:
                                                                       
\begin{align}
    \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , \phi_j \right\rangle = \langle \hat y_{j} &,  v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+2}}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \label{eq: mse slow poisson 1}\\
    &  + v_g(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{{j+2}}) - v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+2}})\label{eq: mse slow poisson 2}\\
    &  +v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j+1}}) - v_g(x_{j+1},y_{j+1},\xi_{{j+2}})\label{eq: mse slow poisson 3}\\
    & + \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]\rangle \label{eq: mse slow poisson 4}
\end{align}
the first term \eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 1} is 0 under expectation because it is a Martingale Difference. The last term \eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 4} is treated in the same way as in \eqref{eq: poisson fast 4} and it has the following bound
\begin{align*}
    \beta \left | \sum_{j=0}^k (1-\mu_g \beta)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat y_{j}  , \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \right\rangle\right |  \\
    \leq {L_{K} \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} |\Xi|(\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g ) \over \mu_g}
\end{align*}

The second term \eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 2} is of order $O( \alpha + \beta)$:
\begin{align*}
     \left | \eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 2}\right | 
     \leq &  \left \| \hat y_{j} \right\| \left \| v_g(x_{j+1}, y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) - v_g(x_{j}, y_{j},\xi_{j+2}) \right\|\\
     \leq & L_{v_g} \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}  (\left \| y_{j+1} - y_j \right\| + \left \| x_{j+1} - x_j \right\| ) \\
     \leq & L_{v_g} \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} (\left \| \alpha f(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} ) \right\| + \left \| \beta g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} ) \right\|)\\
     \leq & L_{v_g} \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}( \alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g  )
\end{align*}
where in the third line we have used the definition of the updates and in the last line we have used the bounds from Lemma~\ref{lemma: bounds}. So we have: 
\begin{align*}
    \left |  \beta \sum_{j=0}^k(1-\beta \mu_g)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , v_g(x_{j+1}, y_{j+1},\xi_{j+2}) - v_g(x_{j}, y_{j},\xi_{j+2}) \right\rangle\right | \leq& \beta \sum_{j=0}^k(1-\beta \mu_g)^{k-j}\left | \eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 2} \right | 
    \\ \leq & {L_{v_g} \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S}( \alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g  )\over \mu_g}
\end{align*}
where in the first inequality we have used the triangular inequality. Moving to the third term \eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 3}. Let $w_j = v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1})$, the term in the sum becomes:
\begin{align*}
    -\eqref{eq: mse slow poisson 3}=\left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_{j+1} - w_j \right\rangle &= \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle -  \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_j \right\rangle \\
    &= \left \langle \hat y_{j+1} +\beta g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle -  \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_j \right\rangle\\
    &= \left \langle \hat y_{j+1}  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle -  \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_j \right\rangle + \left \langle \beta g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle
\end{align*}
                                                                                                                              
We substitute in the sum:
\begin{align}
    \beta \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_{j+1} - w_j \right\rangle &=  \beta \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j}(  \left \langle \hat y_{j+1}  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle -  \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_j \right\rangle) \label{eq: poisson mse slow 1 telescoping}\\ 
     &+ \beta^2 \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j} \left \langle g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle \nonumber
\end{align}
treating the first term \eqref{eq: poisson mse slow 1 telescoping}:
\begin{align*}
     \eqref{eq: poisson mse slow 1 telescoping}=& \beta (1-\beta\mu_g)^{-1}\left \langle \hat y_{k+1}  , w_{k+1} \right\rangle - \beta (1-\beta\mu_g)^k \left \langle \hat y_{0}  , w_{0} \right\rangle\\
     &-\beta^2\mu_g\sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-(j+1)} \left \langle \hat y_{j+1}  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle
\end{align*}
substituting: 
\begin{align}
\left | \beta \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j}\left \langle \hat y_{j}  , w_{j+1} - w_j \right\rangle\right |  =&  \beta \left | (1-\beta\mu_g)^{-1}\left \langle \hat y_{k+1}  , w_{k+1} \right\rangle - (1-\beta\mu_g)^k \left \langle \hat y_{0}  , w_{0} \right\rangle\right |   \label{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 1}\\
     & +\beta^2 \mu_g \left | \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-(j+1)} \left \langle \hat y_{j+1}  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle\right | \label{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 2}\\
     &+ \beta^2 \left | \sum_{j=0}^{k}  (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j} \left \langle g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{j +1} )  , w_{j+1} \right\rangle\right | \label{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 3}
\end{align}
we bound the term \eqref{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 1}:
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 1} &\leq  \beta \mathrm{M}_\xi \left (  2 - \beta \mu_g \over 1 - \beta \mu_g\right ) 
\end{align*}
the second term \eqref{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 2}:
\begin{align*}
   \eqref{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 2} &\leq \beta {\mathrm{M}_\xi \over 1 - \beta \mu_g}
\end{align*}
and the last term \eqref{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 3}:
\begin{align*}
     \eqref{eq: slow poisson telescoping remainder 3} \leq  \beta { \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_g \over \mu_g} 
\end{align*}
where we have used the bounds from Lemma~\ref{lemma: bounds} as we have done in detail for the previous section regarding the fast iterate. The last bound can be written as:
\begin{align*}
        \left | \beta  \mathbb{E} \left [  \sum_{j=0}^{k} (1-\beta\mu_g)^{k-j} \left \langle \hat y_{j}  , \phi_j \right\rangle \right ]\right |  \leq&  \beta \left (  {(L_{v_g} + \mathrm{M}_\xi  L_{K}  |\Xi|) \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_g  \over \mu_g} +   \mathrm{M}_\xi \left ( {3 - \beta \mu_g \over 1 - \beta \mu_g} + {\mathrm{M}_g \over \mu_g} \right ) \right )  \\
        & + \alpha {(L_{v_g} + \mathrm{M}_\xi  L_{K}  |\Xi|) \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_f\over \mu_g}
\end{align*}
which concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lemma: Uniform Bound Riccati}
Let $\varepsilon > 0$ and let $J_{yx} \in \mathbb{R}^{m\times n}, J_{xx}\in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n}, J_{yy} \in \mathbb{R}^{m\times m}, J_{xy} \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times m}$ with $J_{xx}$ being invertible, for every $R_{ric}> 0 $ we define $\varepsilon_{0}^*, \varepsilon_{1}^*$ as follows : 
\begin{align}
    \varepsilon_{0}^* & = {R_{ric} \over  \left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}  (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) ( \left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} +(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}})} ,\label{eq: Riccati eps for the ball}\\
    \varepsilon_{1}^* & = \frac{1}{\left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 \left \|  J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \right)}\label{eq: Riccati eps for the contraction}
\end{align}
with $U_0 = J_{xx}^{-1}J_{xy}$. Then for every $\varepsilon < \min\{\varepsilon_{0}^*, \varepsilon_{1}^*\}$ there exists a matrix $U \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times m}$ that belongs to the closed ball $B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0} = \{U : \left \| U - U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq R_{ric}\}$ that is the unique solution of the following Riccati equation: 
\begin{align} 
     \varepsilon U J_{yx} U
     + J_{xx} U
     - \varepsilon U J_{yy}
     =
     J_{xy} \label{eq: general riccati equation}
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We multiply the Riccati equation \eqref{eq: general riccati equation} by $J_{xx}^{-1}$,since we assumed it is invertible:
\[
\varepsilon J_{xx}^{-1} U J_{yx} U + U - \varepsilon J_{xx}^{-1} U J_{yy} = \underbrace{J_{xx}^{-1} J_{xy}}_{\triangleq U_0}
\]
which gives the fixed point equation:
\begin{equation}
U = U_0 + \varepsilon \left( J_{xx}^{-1} U J_{yy} - J_{xx}^{-1} U J_{yx} U \right) 
\end{equation}
We define $T$ to be the operator of the fixed point equation.
We define the closed ball:
\[
B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0} = \{ U : \left \|  U - U_0  \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq R_{ric} \}
\]
First let's find $\varepsilon_{0}^*$ such that $T(B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}) \subseteq B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}$. Let $M \in B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}$:
\begin{align*}
     T(M) = U_0 + \varepsilon \left( J_{xx}^{-1} M J_{yy} - J_{xx}^{-1} M J_{yx} M \right) 
\end{align*}
calculating the norm 
\begin{align*}
 \left \| T(M) - U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}} &= \varepsilon \left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} M J_{yy} - J_{xx}^{-1} M J_{yx} M   \right\|_{\text{op}}\\
 & \leq \varepsilon (\left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} M J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} +\left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} M J_{yx} M   \right\|_{\text{op}})\\
 & = \varepsilon (\left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} (M - U_0 + U_0) J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} +\left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} (M - U_0 + U_0) J_{yx} (M - U_0 + U_0)   \right\|_{\text{op}})\\
 &\leq \varepsilon (\left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}  (\left \| M - U_0   \right\|_{\text{op}} +\left \|  U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} \\
 &\quad +\left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} (\left \| M - U_0   \right\|_{\text{op}} +\left \|  U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})^2 \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}})\\
 &\leq \varepsilon (\left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}  (R +\left \|  U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} +\left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric}+\left \|  U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})^2 \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}})
\end{align*}
now we condition on $\varepsilon$ to make $T(M) \in B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}$, we need $\varepsilon \leq \varepsilon_0^* $ such that : 
\begin{align*}
    \varepsilon_0^* = {R_{ric} \over  \left \|   J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}  (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) ( \left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} +(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}})}
\end{align*}
Now we prove $T$ is a contraction. Let $U_1, U_2 \in B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}$, we calculate the norm of their difference:
\begin{align*}
\left \|  T(U_1) - T(U_2)  \right\|_{\text{op}} &= \left \|  \varepsilon \left( J_{xx}^{-1} U_1 J_{yy} - J_{xx}^{-1} U_1 J_{yx} U_1 \right) - \varepsilon \left( J_{xx}^{-1} U_2 J_{yy} - J_{xx}^{-1} U_2 J_{yx} U_2 \right)  \right\|_{\text{op}}
\end{align*}
We have:
\[
U_1 A U_1 - U_2 A U_2 = U_1 A U_1 - U_1 A U_2 + U_1 A U_2 - U_2 A U_2 = U_1 A (U_1 - U_2) + (U_1 - U_2) A U_2
\]
We replace:
\begin{align*}
\left \|  T(U_1) - T(U_2)  \right\|_{\text{op}} &= \varepsilon \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \left( (U_1 - U_2) J_{yy} - ( (U_1 - U_2) J_{yx} U_2 + U_1 J_{yx} (U_1 - U_2) ) \right)  \right\|_{\text{op}} \\
\left \|  T(U_1) - T(U_2)  \right\|_{\text{op}} &\leq \varepsilon \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  U_1 - U_2  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + \left \|  J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} ( \left \|  U_1  \right\|_{\text{op}} + \left \|  U_2  \right\|_{\text{op}} ) \right)
\end{align*}
Bounding the norms:
\[
\left \|  U_1  \right\| \leq \left \|  U_1 - U_0  \right\| + \left \|  U_0  \right\|  \leq R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}
\]
\[
\left \|  U_2  \right\| \leq \left \|  U_2 - U_0  \right\| + \left \|  U_0  \right\|  \leq R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}
\]
\[
\left \|  T(U_1) - T(U_2)  \right\| \leq \underbrace{\varepsilon \left( \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 \left \|  J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \right) \right)}_{r(\varepsilon)} \left \|  U_1 - U_2  \right\|_{\text{op}}
\]
To make $r(\varepsilon) < 1$ we need:
\[
\varepsilon < \frac{1}{\left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 \left \|  J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \right)} = \varepsilon_{1}^*
\]
For $\varepsilon < \min\{\varepsilon_{0}^*,\varepsilon_{1}^*, \}$, the solution $U$ of the Riccati equation is unique and is inside the ball $B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}$. It can be obtained by successive application of $T$ on $U_0$. This shows that the uniform bound on $\left \|  U  \right\| \leq R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}$ is proved.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lemma: Uniform Bound Sylvester}
    Let $\varepsilon > 0$ and let $J_{yx} \in \mathbb{R}^{m\times n}, J_{xx}\in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n}, J_{yy} \in \mathbb{R}^{m\times m}, J_{xy} \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times m}$ with $J_{xx}$ being invertible, define $U_0 = J_{xx}^{-1}J_{xy}$, and for all $R_{ric} > 0 $ there exists $\varepsilon^*$ such that for all $\varepsilon < \varepsilon^*$ the matrix $U_\varepsilon \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times m}$ is bounded by $\left \| U_\varepsilon \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}$, we set $R_{syl} = 2 \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}$ and we define   $\varepsilon_{2}^*, \varepsilon_{3}^*$  : 
    \begin{align}
    \varepsilon_{2}^* & = {1  \over  \left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}}  \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}},\label{eq: eps condition sylvester ball}\\
    \varepsilon_{3}^* & = \frac{1}{\left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 \left \|  J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \right)}\label{eq: eps condition sylvester contraction}
\end{align}
then for all $\varepsilon < \min\{\varepsilon^* , \varepsilon_{2}^*, \varepsilon_{3}^* \}$ there exists a matrix  $X\in \mathbb{R}^{m\times n}$ that belongs to the closed ball  $B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}} = \{X : \left \| X \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq \varepsilon R_{syl} \}$ which is the unique solution of the following Sylvester equation: 
\[
X (J_{xx} + \varepsilon U_\varepsilon J_{yx}) - \varepsilon (J_{yy} - J_{yx} U_\varepsilon) X = - \varepsilon J_{yx}
\]
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
To make the notation lighter we drop $\varepsilon$ from $U_\varepsilon$ such that $U = U _\varepsilon$. We multiply the Sylvester equation by $J_{xx}^{-1}$ on the right:
\[
X + \varepsilon X U J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1} - \varepsilon J_{yy} X J_{xx}^{-1} + \varepsilon J_{yx} U X J_{xx}^{-1} = - \varepsilon J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1}
\]
which gives the fixed point equation:
\[
X = \underbrace{\varepsilon \left( J_{yy} X J_{xx}^{-1} - X U J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1} - J_{yx} U X J_{xx}^{-1} - J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1} \right)}_{T(X)}
\]
We define the ball $B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}} = \{ X : \left \|  X  \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq \varepsilon R_{syl} \}$ where $R_{syl} = 2 \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}$ (This choice is explained in the next lines). Let $M \in B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}}$ and $U \in B^{U_0}_{R_{ric}}$:
\begin{align*}
    \left \| T(M) \right\|_{\text{op}} &= \varepsilon \left \| J_{yy} M J_{xx}^{-1} - M U J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1} - J_{yx} U M J_{xx}^{-1} - J_{yx} J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \\
    & \leq \varepsilon (\left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  M \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2\left \| M \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  U \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}})\\
    & = \varepsilon ( \left \|  M \right\|_{\text{op}}(\left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}}  \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 \left \|  U \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}) + \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}})\\
    & \leq \varepsilon R_{syl} {  \varepsilon R_{syl} (\left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}}  \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}) + \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \over R_{syl}}
\end{align*}
a first condition to set is that 
\[
{  \varepsilon R_{syl} (\left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}}  \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}) + \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \over R_{syl}} \leq 1 
\]
to make the operator $T$ maps into $B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}}$ which is equivalent to 
\[
{  \varepsilon  } < {R_{syl} - \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \over R_{syl} (\left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}}  \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}})}
\]
where we extract two conditions , the first is that $R_{syl} > \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}$ since $\varepsilon$ is strictly positive, here we justify the choice of the radius $R_{syl} = 2 \left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}$, once we have done that we can have a condition on $\varepsilon \leq \varepsilon_{2}^*$ to ensure that image of $T$ stays in the ball $B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}}$ such that:
\begin{align*}
    \varepsilon_{2}^* ={1  \over  \left \| J_{yy} \right\|_{\text{op}}  \left \| J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \left \|  J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}}
\end{align*}
Now we move to the contraction part of $T$. Let $X_1, X_2 \in B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}}$:
\begin{align*}
\left \|  T(X_1) - T(X_2)  \right\|_{\text{op}} &\leq \varepsilon \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + \left \|  U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + \left \|  J_{yx} U  \right\|_{\text{op}} \right) \left \|  X_1 - X_2  \right\|_{\text{op}} \\
&\leq \varepsilon \left \|  J_{xx}^{-1}  \right\|_{\text{op}} \left( \left \|  J_{yy}  \right\|_{\text{op}} + 2 \left \|  J_{yx}  \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) \right) \left \|  X_1 - X_2  \right\|_{\text{op}}
\end{align*}
The condition for the contraction is the same as the Riccati equation
\[
\varepsilon_{3}^* = \varepsilon_{1}^*
\]
then by Banach fixed point theorem, the map $T$ has a fixed point solution $X$ that is unique inside the ball $B_{\varepsilon R_{syl}}$ for every $\varepsilon < \min\{\varepsilon_{2}^*, \varepsilon_{3}^*\}$.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lemma: Lyapunov's fast contraction}
Let $-J_{xx}$ be a Hurwitz matrix in $\mathbb R ^{d\times d}$, $J_{yx}\in \mathbb R ^{n \times d}$ such that $\left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq M_J$ for some positive constant $M_J$, $\mathrm L \in \mathbb R ^{d\times d}$ a symmetric positive definite matrix a solution to the following Lyapunov equation:
\begin{align}
J_{xx}^T \mathrm L + \mathrm L J_{xx} = I. \label{eq: lyapunov equation fast}
\end{align}
Let $R_{ric}> 0 $, there exists $\alpha^* = \alpha^*(R_{ric})$ where $\alpha^*(R_{ric})$ is defined by:
\begin{align}
    \alpha^*(R_{ric}) = {1\over 4 \left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}}(\left \|  J_{xx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2+ {\varepsilon_{4}^*}^2 \kappa(L)(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})^2\left \| J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2)} \label{eq: alpha condition fast Lyapunov}
\end{align}
such that for every $  0 < \alpha \leq \alpha^*$ and for every $\beta$ such that ${\beta \over \alpha} < \min\{\varepsilon_{0}^*, \varepsilon_{1}^*,\varepsilon_{5}^*\}$ where $\varepsilon_{0}^*, \varepsilon_{1}^*,\varepsilon_{5}^*$ depend on $R_{ric}$ and are fully defined in \eqref{eq: Riccati eps for the ball}, \eqref{eq: Riccati eps for the contraction}, \eqref{eq: eps condition for fast lyapunov contraction},
we define the matrix $U_{\beta \over \alpha} \in \mathbb R ^{d\times n}$ to be bounded by $\|U_{\beta \over \alpha }\| \leq R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}$, then we have: 
\begin{align*}
    \left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U_{\beta\over\alpha} J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}  \leq e^{-c\alpha }
\end{align*}
where $c = (8 \left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Under the Lyapunov norm:
\begin{align*}
\left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2 &= \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L} = 1} u^T (I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx})^T\mathrm L(I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}) u \\
&= \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L} = 1} \{ u^T (L - \alpha J_{xx}^T\mathrm L- \beta (U J_{yx})^T\mathrm L- \alpha\mathrm LJ_{xx} + \alpha^2 J_{xx}^T\mathrm LJ_{xx} \\
&\hspace{1.6cm}+ \alpha \beta J_{yx}^T U^T\mathrm LJ_{xx} - \beta\mathrm L U J_{yx} + \alpha \beta J_{xx}^T\mathrm L U J_{yx} + \beta^2 J_{yx}^T U^T\mathrm L U J_{yx}) u \} \\
&= \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L}=1} \{ u^T\mathrm L u - \alpha u^T(J_{xx}^T\mathrm L +\mathrm L J_{xx})u + \alpha^2 u^T J_{xx}^T\mathrm L J_{xx} u \\
&\hspace{1.6cm} - \beta u^T (\underbrace{J_{yx}^T U^T\mathrm L +\mathrm L U J_{yx}}_{A_1})u + \alpha \beta u^T (\underbrace{J_{yx}^T U^T\mathrm L J_{xx} + J_{xx}^T\mathrm L U J_{yx}}_{A_2}) u \\
&\hspace{1.6cm}+ \beta^2 u^T (J_{yx}^T U^T\mathrm L U J_{yx}) u \} \\
&= 1 + \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L}=1} \{ - \alpha u^T u + \alpha^2 u^T J_{xx}^T\mathrm L J_{xx} u - \beta u^T A_1 u + \alpha \beta u^T A_2 u \\
&\hspace{2.2cm}+ \beta^2 u^T (J_{yx}^T U^T\mathrm L U J_{yx}) u \} \\
&\leq 1 + \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L}=1} \{ - \alpha u^T u\} + \alpha^2 \left \|  J_{xx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2+ \beta^2 \left \|  U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2 + \alpha \beta \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_2 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}  \\
&\quad + \beta \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L} = 1} -u^T A_1 u\\
&\leq 1 - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \alpha^2 \left \|  J_{xx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2+ \beta^2 \left \|  U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2\\
&\quad  - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \alpha \beta \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_2 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}  + \beta \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L} = 1} -u^T A_1 u
\end{align*}
We have used the fact that $\inf_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L}=1} \{ u^T u \} = \left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}}^{-1} =(2\left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + (2\left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$.We will use the symmetry of $A_1$ to bound the last term such that 
\begin{align*}
    \beta \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L} = 1} -u^T A_1 u \leq  \beta \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm L} = 1} |u^T A_1 u| = \beta \sup_{\left \| z \right\| = 1} |z^T \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_1 \mathrm L^{-1/2} z| = \beta \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_1 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}
\end{align*}
we have 
\begin{align*}
\left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2 
&\leq 1 - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \alpha^2 (\left \|  J_{xx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2+ {\beta^2\over \alpha ^2} \left \|  U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2)\\
&\quad  - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \beta  \alpha \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_2 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}  + \beta \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_1 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}
\end{align*}
Since $U$ is a solution of the Riccati equation \eqref{eq:riccati-equation} then by Lemma \ref{lemma: Uniform Bound Riccati} for all $R_{ric}>0$ there exists $\varepsilon_{0}^*$ and $\varepsilon_{1}^*$ defined in \eqref{eq: Riccati eps for the ball} and \eqref{eq: Riccati eps for the contraction}, respectively, such that for ${\beta \over \alpha} < \varepsilon_{4}^* \triangleq \min\{\varepsilon_{0}^*,\varepsilon_{1}^*\}  $ we have $\left \| U \right\| \leq R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}$. We apply the result of lemma:
\begin{align*}
    \left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2 
&\leq 1 - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \alpha^2 (\left \|  J_{xx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2+ {\varepsilon_{4}^*}^2 \kappa(L)(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})^2\left \| J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2)\\
&\quad  - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \beta  \alpha \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_2 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}  + \beta \left \|  \mathrm L^{-1/2} A_1 \mathrm L^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}\\ 
& \leq 1 - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \alpha^2 (\left \|  J_{xx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2+ {\varepsilon_{4}^*}^2 \kappa(L)(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})^2\left \| J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2)\\
&\quad  - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + 2 \beta  \alpha \kappa (L)(\left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| J_{xx} \right\|_{\text{op}}(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}))  \\
& \quad + 2 \beta \kappa(L)^{1/2}(M_J (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})) 
\end{align*}
First, we look for values of $\alpha$ such that the first line is less than one to get the contraction property. This is true for the choice of $\alpha \leq \alpha^*(R_{ric})$ defined in the body of the lemma so we have:
\begin{align*}
    \left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2
& \leq 1 - c'\alpha  \\
&\quad  - \alpha (2\left \| \mathrm L  \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + 2 \beta  \alpha^* \kappa (L)(\left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| J_{xx} \right\|_{\text{op}}(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}))  \\
& \quad + 2 \beta \kappa(L)^{1/2}(M_J(R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})) 
\end{align*}
with $c' = (4 \left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} $. Second, we need the term in the last two lines to be negative so this will imply a condition of the type: 
\begin{align}
    {\beta \over \alpha } \leq \varepsilon_{5}^* \triangleq {1 \over 2 \left \| \mathrm L \right\|_{\text{op}} (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}}) (2   \alpha^* \kappa (L)\left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| J_{xx} \right\|_{\text{op}}  + 2 \kappa(L)^{1/2} M_J )} \label{eq: eps condition for fast lyapunov contraction}
\end{align}
Once this is established we have:
\begin{align*}
\left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}^2  \leq 1 -c' \alpha \leq e^{-c\alpha }
\end{align*}
this implies that: 
\begin{align*}
\left \|  I - \alpha J_{xx} - \beta U J_{yx}  \right\|_{\mathrm L}  \leq e^{-{c'\over 2}\alpha }
\end{align*}
which establishes the proof.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lemma: Lyapunov's slow contraction}
Let $-\Delta$ be a Hurwitz matrix in $\mathbb R ^{n\times n}$, $J_{yx}\in \mathbb R ^{n \times m}$ such that $\left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq M_J$ for some positive constant $M_J$, $\mathrm G \in \mathbb R ^{n\times n}$ a symmetric positive definite matrix a solution to the following Lyapunov equation:
\begin{align}
\Delta^T \mathrm G + \mathrm G \Delta = I. \label{eq: lyapunov equation slow}
\end{align}
Let $R_{ric} = {1\over 4 \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm G)} M_J }$, we define $\beta^* = \beta^*(R_{ric})$ where $\beta^*(R_{ric})$ is defined by:
\begin{align}
    \beta^* = {1 \over 4 \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}} ( 2\kappa(G) \left \| \Delta \right\|_{\text{op}} M_JR_{ric}+  \left \| \Delta  \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 + \kappa(G) M_J^2 R_{ric}^2)}\label{eq: condition beta contraction}
\end{align}
for all $\alpha >0 $ and $\beta \leq \beta^*$ with ${\beta \over \alpha} < \min\{\varepsilon_{0}^*,\varepsilon_{1}^*\}$ where $\varepsilon_{0}^*,\varepsilon_{1}^*$ are dependent on $R_{ric}$ and defined in \eqref{eq: Riccati eps for the ball} and \eqref{eq: Riccati eps for the contraction}, the matrix $U_{\beta \over \alpha} \in B_{R_{ric}}^{U_0}$ is a solution of the Riccati equation defined in \eqref{eq: general riccati equation} with $\varepsilon = {\beta \over \alpha }$ and $U_0 = J_{xx}^{-1} J_{xy}$, and we have:
\begin{align*}
    \left \| I - \beta (\Delta-  J_{yx}(U_{\beta\over \alpha } - U_0)) \right\|_{\mathrm G}  \leq e^{-d\beta }
\end{align*}
where $d = (8 \left \| \mathrm G \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Taking the Lyapunov norm 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| I - \beta (\Delta-  J_{yx}(U - U_0)) \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 &= \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm G}=1}\{u^T (I - \beta \Delta- \beta J_{yx}(U - U_0))^TG (I - \beta \Delta- \beta J_{yx}(U - U_0))u 
    \} \\
    & =\sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm G}=1} \{ u^T Gu 
    - \beta u^T G  \Delta u 
    - \beta u^T G  J_{yx}(U - U_0)u 
    -\beta  u^T \Delta^TG u 
    \\
    & \hspace{1.5cm}+ \beta^2 u^T  \Delta^TG  \Delta u  + \beta^2 u^T  \Delta^TG J_{yx}(U - U_0)u 
    \\
    & \hspace{1.5cm}- \beta  u^T (U - U_0)^TJ_{yx}^TG u  + \beta^2 u^T  (U - U_0)^TJ_{yx}^TG \Delta u\\
    &\hspace{1.5cm} + \beta^2 u^T (U - U_0)^TJ_{yx}^TG  J_{yx}(U - U_0)u \}\\
    & =1 + \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm G}=1} \{  
    - \beta u^T u 
    - 2\beta u^T G  J_{yx}(U - U_0) u  + 2\beta^2 u^T  \Delta^TG J_{yx}(U - U_0)u 
     \\
     & \hspace{2.1cm}+ \beta^2 u^T  \Delta^TG  \Delta u+ \beta^2 u^T (U - U_0)^TJ_{yx}^TG  J_{yx}(U - U_0)u \}\\
     & \leq 1  - \beta \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}}^{-1}
    + 2\beta\sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm G}=1} \{-  u^T G  J_{yx}(U - U_0) u\}
     \\
    & \quad+ 2\beta^2 \sup_{\left \| u \right\|_{\mathrm G}=1} \{u^T  \Delta^TG J_{yx}(U - U_0)u \}
     + \beta^2 \left \| \Delta  \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 + \beta^2 \left \| J_{yx}(U - U_0) \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 \\
     & \leq 1  - \beta \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}}^{-1}
    + 2\beta \left \|  G^{1/2}  J_{yx}(U - U_0) G^{-1/2} \right\|_{\text{op}}
     \\
    & \quad+ 2\beta^2  \left \|  G^{-1/2} \Delta^TG J_{yx}(U - U_0)G^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}
     + \beta^2 \left \| \Delta  \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 + \beta^2 \left \| J_{yx}(U - U_0) \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 \\
    & \leq 1  - \beta \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}}^{-1}
    + 2\beta  \kappa(G)^{1/2} M_J \left \|  U - U_0  \right\|_{\text{op}}
     \\
    & \quad+ 2\beta^2  \left \|  G^{-1/2} \Delta^TG J_{yx}(U - U_0)G^{-1/2}  \right\|_{\text{op}}
     + \beta^2 \left \| \Delta  \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 + \beta^2 \left \| J_{yx}(U - U_0) \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 
\end{align*}
Here we set the radius of convergence of the Riccati equation to calibrate for the negative term in the first line of the last inequality above. We want:
\begin{align*}
    2  \kappa(G)^{1/2} M_J \left \|  U - U_0  \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq  (2\left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}
\end{align*}
which is equivalent to:
\begin{align*}
 \left \|  U - U_0  \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq  {1\over 4 \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}} \kappa(G)^{1/2} M_J } = R_{ric}
\end{align*}
So under this condition we have 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| I - \beta (\Delta-  J_{yx}(U - U_0)) \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 
    & \leq 1  - \beta (2\left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1} + \beta^2 ( 2\kappa(G) \left \| \Delta \right\|_{\text{op}} M_J R_{ric} +  \left \| \Delta  \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 + \kappa(G) M_J^2 R_{ric}^2)
\end{align*}
So the contraction is established for $\beta \leq \beta^*$ where $\beta^*$ is defined in the body of the lemma and for $d' = (4 \left \| G \right\|_{\text{op}})^{-1}$ we have 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| I - \beta (\Delta-  J_{yx}(U - U_0)) \right\|_{\mathrm G}^2 
    & \leq 1  - d\beta  \leq e^{-d'\beta }
\end{align*}
this implies that: 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| I - \beta (\Delta-  J_{yx}(U - U_0)) \right\|_{\mathrm G}  \leq e^{-{d'\over 2}\beta }
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lemma:bias-poisson} Taking the sequence $\{x_k,y_k\}_{k\geq 0}$ defined by \eqref{eq: TTSA update} and under assumptions A.\ref{assumption: Boundedness}-\ref{assumption: All Kernel Properties} and B.\ref{assump : derivability}. Assume also that the step sizes $\alpha$ and $\beta$ satisfy $\alpha<\alpha^*$, $\beta < \beta^*$ and ${\beta \over \alpha }< \omega^*$ such that $\alpha^*,\beta^*, \omega^*$ are defined in \eqref{eq: alpha star condition for bias thm},\eqref{eq: beta star condition for bias thm},\eqref{eq: omega star condition for bias thm} then, the term \eqref{eq:fast-bias-term-3} is bounded by:
\begin{align*}
  \eqref{eq:fast-bias-term-3} \leq & \alpha A_1 +\beta (A_2 + B_1)+ \alpha ^2 A_3+ \beta^2 B_2 + \alpha \beta (A_4 + B_3) +{\beta ^2 \over \alpha} B_4
\end{align*}
in addition the term \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-3} is bounded by: 
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq:slow-bias-term-3} \leq & \alpha (C_2 + D_4) + \beta (C_1 + D_1 )+ \alpha \beta (C_4 + D_3) + \beta^2 (C_3 + D_2)
\end{align*}
where the constants are independent of $\alpha$ and $\beta$ and are detailed in the proof.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
We start with bounding the first term: 
\begin{align}
    \alpha \left \| \sum_{j=0}^k (I-\alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta})^{k-j}\mathbb{E} \left [ \psi_j \right ] \right\| \label{eq: sum of fast contracted markovian sum 1}
\end{align}
In Lemma \ref{lemma : poisson mse} we have seen that there exists a function $v_f$ the solution of the Poisson equation \eqref{eq: poisson solution fast}, which allowed us to write:
\begin{align*}
   \mathbb{E} \left [ \psi_s \right ] = \mathbb{E} \left [ v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi_{{s}+1}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] \right ]
\end{align*}
we could write the term inside the expectation as:
\begin{align}
    \psi_s =& v_f(x_s, y_s, \xi_{s+2}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s+1}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] \label{eq: poisson fast 1 (bias)}\\
    &+  v_f(x_{s+1}, y_{s+1}, \xi_{s+2}) -  v_f(x_s, y_s, \xi_{s+2}) \label{eq: poisson fast 2 (bias)}\\
    &  + v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi_{{s}+1}) - v_f(x_{s+1}, y_{s+1}, \xi_{s+2})\label{eq: poisson fast 3 (bias)}\\
    & + \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s+1}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] \label{eq: poisson fast 4 (bias)}
\end{align}
where the first term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 1 (bias)} is a Martingale Difference Sequence and we denote $K_s \triangleq K_{(x_{s},y_{s})}{}$. By taking the expectation, we get:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{E}[v_f(x_s, y_s, \xi_{s+2}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s+1}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] ] =&  \mathbb{E} \big [ \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s+1}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] \\  & - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s+1}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] \big] \\
    =& 0
\end{align*}
moving to the second term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 2 (bias)} and for legibility, we set $v_f(x_s,y_s, \xi_{s+2}) = v_ s$ and $v_f(x_{s+1},y_{s+1}, \xi_{s+2}) = v_{s+1}$ we have:
\begin{align*}
    \alpha \left \| \sum_{s=0}^k (I- \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta})^{k-s} (v_s - v_{s+1}) \right\|\leq & \alpha  \sum_{s=0}^k \left \| I- \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|^{k-s} \left \| v_s - v_{s+1} \right\|\\
    \leq & \alpha\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \sum_{s=0}^k e^{-c\alpha (k-s)}\left \| v_s - v_{s+1} \right\|\\
    \leq & \alpha\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f} \sum_{s=0}^k e^{-c\alpha (k-s)}(\left \| x_s - x_{s+1} \right\|+ \left \| y_s - y_{s+1} \right\|)\\
    \leq &  \alpha\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}L_{v_f}  (\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g) \sum_{s=0}^k e^{-c\alpha s} \\
    \leq & \alpha\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f} (\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g)  ( 1 + \int _0^\infty  e^{-c\alpha t}dt)\\
    = &  \alpha c^{-1}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f}  \mathrm{M}_f   +  \beta c^{-1}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f}  \mathrm{M}_g \\
    & + \alpha^2 \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f} \mathrm{M}_f +  \alpha \beta \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f}  \mathrm{M}_g 
\end{align*}
Next, we handle the pseudo-telescoping term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 3 (bias)}, but before that we set $v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi_{{s}+1})= v_s$, $v_f(x_{s+1}, y_{s+1}, \xi_{s+2}) = v_{s+1}$, $J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} = J_{xx}+ {\beta \over \alpha }U J_{yx}$ and $A_{\alpha,\beta} = I - \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta}$. 
From the Neumann series convergence, $A_{\alpha,\beta}$ is invertible if we have a condition of the type \[\alpha \left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} < 1 \]
since we are assuming 
\begin{align}
    \alpha < {1\over \left \| J_{xx} \right\|_{\text{op}} + (R_{ric} + \left \| U_0 \right\|_{\text{op}})\left \| J_{yx} \right\|_{\text{op}}} \label{eq: alpha for invertible fast bias}
\end{align}
then the condition is satisfied and $A_{\alpha,\beta}$ is invertible and the inverse can be given by the Neumann series $A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} = \sum_{k=0}^{\infty} \left ( \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta}\right ) ^k $. However, we will not need the explicit form of the inverse, so we use it directly:
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{s=0}^k A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s}(v_s- v_{s+1}) =& \sum_{s=0}^k A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s}v_s- A_{\alpha,\beta} A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-(s+1)}v_{s+1}\\
    =&\sum_{s=0}^k A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s}v_s- A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-(s+1)}v_{s+1}+ \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \sum_{s=0}^{k}  A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s}v_{s+1}\\
    = & A_{\alpha,\beta}^kv_0 - A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1}v_{k+1} + \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \sum_{s=0}^{k}  A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s}v_{s+1}
\end{align*}
taking the norm:
\begin{align*}
    \alpha \left \| \sum_{s=0}^k A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s}(v_s- v_{s+1}) \right\| \leq  &\alpha^2 \left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \sum_{s=0}^{k}  \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-s} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| v_{s+1} \right\| + \alpha \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^k \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| v_0 \right\| \\&+ \alpha \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| v_{k+1} \right\| \\
    \leq &\alpha^2 \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi\sum_{s=0}^{k}e^{-c\alpha s}+ \alpha e^{-c\alpha k} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\mathrm{M}_\xi\\& + \alpha \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \mathrm{M}_\xi \\
    \leq & \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi\left ( \alpha^2 + {\alpha \over c }\right ) + \alpha e^{-c\alpha k} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \mathrm{M}_\xi \\
    &+ \alpha \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \mathrm{M}_\xi \\
    = & \alpha^2 \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi + \alpha e^{-c\alpha k} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \mathrm{M}_\xi\\
    &+ \alpha c^{-1}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi + \alpha \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \mathrm{M}_\xi 
\end{align*}
where in the second inequality we used $\left \| D \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| D \right\|_{\mathrm L}$. 

The last term \eqref{eq: poisson fast 4 (bias)} is capturing the drift in the kernel and is bounded in the same way as \eqref{eq: poisson fast 4} by 
\begin{align*}
    \left \| \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s+1}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{s}(\xi_{{s+1}}, \cdot)}[v_f(x_{s},y_{s},\xi')] \right\| \leq \mathrm{M}_\xi \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} L_{K} |\Xi| (\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g)
\end{align*}
which yields the bound 
\begin{align*}
    \alpha \left \|  \sum_{s=0}^k (I- \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta})^{k-s}\eqref{eq: poisson fast 4 (bias)} \right\| \leq \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_\xi L_{K} |\Xi|c^{-1}(\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g)
\end{align*}

We pack all the bounds together, under our assumptions on the step sizes there is $C_A, C_J$ that satisfy $\left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq C_A$ and  $\left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}} \leq C_J$ so we have: 
\begin{align*}
    \eqref{eq: sum of fast contracted markovian sum 1} \leq & \alpha \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \underbrace{\left ( c^{-1} \mathrm{M}_f (L_{v_f}+  \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_\xi L_{K} |\Xi|) +\mathrm{M}_\xi\left ( 1+C_A \left ( c^{-1}C_J+\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}^{-1} \right ) \right ) \right ) }_{\triangleq A_1}
    \\& + \beta \underbrace{\left ( c^{-1}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \mathrm{M}_g(L_{v_f}+  \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_\xi L_{K} |\Xi|)\right ) }_{\triangleq A_2}  + \alpha ^2 \underbrace{\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left (  L_{v_f} \mathrm{M}_f  + C_J C_A\mathrm{M}_\xi\right ) }_{\triangleq A_3}\\
    & + \alpha \beta \underbrace{\left (  \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} L_{v_f}  \mathrm{M}_g\right )  }_{\triangleq A_4}
\end{align*}
The second part of \eqref{eq:fast-bias-term-3} concerns bounding the term: 
\begin{align}
    \beta \left \| \sum_{j=0}^k (I - \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta})^{k-j}  U\mathbb{E} \left [ \varphi_j \right ] \right\| \label{eq: sum of fast contracted markovian sum 2}
\end{align}
that is similar to the first one, except that now we have a multiplication by $\beta$ and the deviations concern the dynamics of the slow variable. There exists a function $v_g$ solution of the Poisson equation: 
\begin{align*}
    v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]= \varphi_j
\end{align*}
so we can write: 
\begin{align}
    \varphi_j =& v_g(x_j, y_j, \xi_{j+2}) - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \label{eq: poisson slow 1 (bias)}\\
    &+  v_g(x_{j+1}, y_{j+1}, \xi_{j+2}) -  v_g(x_j, y_j, \xi_{j+2}) \label{eq: poisson slow 2 (bias)}\\
    &  + v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1}) - v_g(x_{j+1}, y_{j+1}, \xi_{j+2}) \label{eq: poisson slow 3 (bias)}\\
    & +\mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j+1}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')]  - \mathbb{E}_{\xi' \sim K_{j}(\xi_{{j+1}}, \cdot)}[v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi')] \label{eq: poisson slow 4 (bias)}
\end{align}
where term \eqref{eq: poisson slow 1 (bias)} is an MDS, under expectation it will equal zero. The second term \eqref{eq: poisson slow 2 (bias)}, similarly to the previous part, we set $v_g(x_j,y_j, \xi_{j+2}) = v_j$ and $v_g(x_{j+1},y_{j+1}, \xi_{j+2}) = v_{j+1}$ we have 

\begin{align*}
    \beta \left \| \sum_{j=0}^k (I - \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta})^{k-j}  U(v_j - v_{j+1}) \right\|\leq & \beta^2  \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}L_{v_g}  \mathrm{M}_g + \alpha \beta  \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}L_{v_g}  \mathrm{M}_f  \\
    &+ {\beta^2  \over c\alpha } \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}L_{v_g}  \mathrm{M}_g+ \beta  c^{-1} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}L_{v_g}  \mathrm{M}_f
\end{align*}
for the third term \eqref{eq: poisson slow 3 (bias)} it will follow the same bound as the one used in \eqref{eq: poisson fast 3 (bias)}, we just need to multiply it by $\beta$, we set $v_g(x_{j},y_{j},\xi_{{j}+1})= v_j$, $v_g(x_{j+1}, y_{j+1}, \xi_{j+2}) = v_{j+1}$:
\begin{align*}
    \beta \left \| \sum_{j=0}^k A_{\alpha,\beta}^{k-j}U(v_j- v_{j+1}) \right\| \leq  & \alpha \beta \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi + \beta e^{-c\alpha k} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi\\
    &+ \beta c^{-1}\sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)}\left \| J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta} \right\|_{\text{op}}\left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi + \beta \left \| A_{\alpha,\beta}^{-1} \right\|_{\text{op}} \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}}\mathrm{M}_\xi 
\end{align*}
Finally, we bound the term  
\begin{align*}
    \beta \left \|  \sum_{s=0}^k (I- \alpha J_{xx}^{\alpha, \beta})^{k-s} U \eqref{eq: poisson slow 4 (bias)} \right\| \leq {\beta \over \alpha } \left \| U \right\|_{\text{op}} \sqrt{\kappa(\mathrm L)} \mathrm{M}_{\mathcal S} \mathrm{M}_\xi L_{K} |\Xi|c^{-1}(\alpha \mathrm{M}_f + \beta \mathrm{M}_g)
\end{align*}
